**`POST /v1/github/sign-in`** starts gh's own sign-in, or answers the one under way: `{"url": "https://github.com/login/device", "userCode": "AB12-CD34", "expiresAt": "…"}`. The app opens `url` and the person types `userCode` there (gh also puts it on its machine's clipboard).

- It runs `gh auth login --hostname github.com --git-protocol https --web --skip-ssh-key`, unboxed (it must reach the Keychain and the network), by its absolute path, with obid's kept environment (no token, no agent variable), the person's `HOME` (where gh keeps its settings), `GH_BROWSER` and `BROWSER` set to `true` (nothing opens on the runner), prompts off, and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` a file in the sign-in's own folder (`home/tmp/github-sign-in-…`, removed when it ends), so gh never changes the person's global git settings. obid needs no `gh auth setup-git`: it gives git gh's login itself (obid only: publishing).
- obid reads what gh prints only for its one-time code (`! One-time code (AB12-CD34) …` or `! First copy your one-time code: AB12-CD34`); nothing else is kept, answered or logged. The page is always GitHub's device page.
- One at a time. It ends when gh does (signed in, or not), after 15 minutes (a device code's life), with `DELETE`, or when obid stops; its process group is killed with it. `502` when gh gave no code within 30 seconds; `503` without a gh.

**`DELETE /v1/github/sign-in`** ends the sign-in under way, if any: `204`.

### obid only: Tetrix

The owner connects Tetrix once, in Oby, and every agent on every account (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor) uses it with no sign-in of its own (the owner's decision of 2026-10-18, AGENTS.md). obid is Tetrix's OAuth client and holds the login; agents reach Tetrix only through obid, with a credential that works for their own job and dies with it. None of these endpoints is on the gateway's allowlist, and no other Oby can reach them: a paired phone never signs the Mac in to Tetrix (the iPhone shows the state through the accounts' `tetrix` and the jobs' `tetrix`).

**`GET /v1/tetrix`** answers:

```json
{"connected": true, "url": "https://tetrix.example/mcp", "preset": "dogfood",
 "presets": [{"id": "dogfood", "name": "Tetrix Dogfood", "url": "https://tetrix.example/mcp"}],
 "scopes": ["graph:read", "sessions:write", "hitl:write", "offline_access"],
 "connectedAt": "2026-10-18T14:02:11Z", "signIn": null, "error": null}
```

- `connected`: obid holds a login for `url` that it can use or refresh. `url` is the MCP server it signed in to (or last tried), `preset` the preset's id or null for a URL the owner typed. `presets`: what Settings offers before a URL field; today only `dogfood`, "Tetrix Dogfood".
- `scopes`: what the authorization server granted, or null when signed out. `connectedAt`: when the sign-in finished, or null.
- `signIn`: the sign-in under way, as `POST` answers it, or null. `error`: why the last sign-in or refresh failed, in a sentence (`Tetrix signed Oby out: sign in again.`), or null; it clears on the next sign-in.
- No token, code, verifier or client secret is ever in an answer, an event, a log line, a report or `config.json`.

**`POST /v1/tetrix/sign-in`** with `{"preset": "dogfood"}` or `{"url": "https://<host>/mcp"}` starts a sign-in and answers `{"url": "https://<authorization server>/…/authorize?…", "server": "https://<authorization server>/…", "expiresAt": "…"}`. `server` is the authorization server's issuer (where the person signs in), as the MCP server's metadata named it and its own metadata confirmed; `url` is always on its host (obid refuses a server whose authorization endpoint is elsewhere, `502`). The app opens `url` in the person's browser; when they finish there, the browser lands on obid's loopback page, which says `Oby is signed in to Tetrix. You can close this tab.` The app follows it with `GET /v1/tetrix` (every 2 seconds while `signIn` isn't null) or the `tetrix` event.

- The URL follows a connection's rules (Connection, above): HTTPS, with no login, query or fragment; plain HTTP only on 127.0.0.1 or localhost. Anything else, or an unknown preset, is `422` naming what's wrong.
- How obid finds the authorization server (the MCP authorization spec): the server's protected-resource metadata (where an unauthenticated call's `401` says, `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer … resource_metadata="…"`, else `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/<path>`, then at the root) gives `authorization_servers`, `resource` and `scopes_supported`; the server's metadata is RFC 8414's (`/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server/<path>`), else OpenID discovery (`<issuer>/.well-known/openid-configuration`, then `/.well-known/openid-configuration/<path>`), whose `issuer` must be the one asked for. Redirects aren't followed. When none answers JSON, `502` says so.
- The client: a public client with PKCE (S256) and no secret. A preset may name its client id; otherwise obid registers one by dynamic client registration (`registration_endpoint`, `token_endpoint_auth_method` `none`, redirect `http://127.0.0.1/callback`) once per authorization server and keeps its id. Neither: `502`.
- The metadata must be for the server obid was given (RFC 9728 3.3): its `resource` has the URL's origin and its path, or a prefix of it at a `/`; else `502` and no sign-in. That `resource` is the resource indicator obid sends. Plain-HTTP endpoints on 127.0.0.1 are taken only when the URL itself is on 127.0.0.1. Answers from the sign-in server are read up to 256 KB.
- Scopes: the resource's `scopes_supported`, else `graph:read sessions:write hitl:write`; plus `offline_access`, so obid can refresh. The request carries `resource` (RFC 8707), the `state` and the PKCE challenge.
- The redirect is `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/callback` on a port obid opens for this sign-in alone, on 127.0.0.1 only. It answers that one path, takes the first request whose `state` matches, and then closes; a request whose `state` doesn't match gets `400` and changes nothing.
- One at a time: a second `POST` ends the one under way (and waits up to 5 seconds for its page to close, so a registered client's port is free again) and starts another. A sign-in ends when the token exchange does, after 10 minutes, with `DELETE`, or when obid stops.
- The login (access token, refresh token, expiry) goes into obid's vault as `oby.tetrix.access` and `oby.tetrix.refresh` (a connection can't name an `oby.` secret), so it lives where the vault lives (the login Keychain on the Mac app's runner home, else `home/vault/secrets.json`) and is taken out of every answer, log line and push like every vault secret (obid only: the vault). Everything else (`url`, the client id, the endpoints, the expiry, the granted scopes) is in `home/tetrix.json` (0600).
- obid refreshes the access token itself when it has less than a minute left, or when Tetrix answers `401`, and keeps the rotated refresh token. A refresh Tetrix refuses (`invalid_grant`) signs Oby out (`error` says so).

**`DELETE /v1/tetrix/sign-in`** ends the sign-in under way, if any: `204`.

**`DELETE /v1/tetrix`** signs Oby out: it asks the authorization server to revoke the refresh token when it offers `revocation_endpoint` (and goes on if that fails), removes both vault secrets, and keeps `url` and `preset`: `204`. Jobs that are running lose Tetrix at their next call (`Oby isn't signed in to Tetrix.`).

**The `tetrix` event** comes on `/v1/events` with the same body as `GET /v1/tetrix` whenever any of its fields change.

**What agents get.** While Oby is signed in, every owner's job's agent gets one MCP server named `tetrix` that is obid itself: `POST /v1/agent/tetrix/{job}` on obid's port (on Linux, through slirp4netns's 10.0.2.2, as for the broker), with the job's token (the broker's: Connection, above), which works for that job alone, only while it runs, and dies with it. Claude Code gets it in its `--mcp-config` file and one `--allowedTools` rule per tool (`mcp__tetrix__<tool>`); Codex as `mcp_servers.tetrix` with the token in its environment (`bearer_token_env_var`, as the broker); Cursor in `session/new`'s `mcpServers` when it says `mcpCapabilities.http`. The agent never sees Oby's Tetrix token, and Tetrix sees obid's token, never the job's.

- `tools/list` lists only the tools agents may call (plugins/tetrix/README.md: the graph reads, the earlier sessions' digests and `save_session`); `tools/call` of any other (`resume_session`, admin, write and diagnostics tools) is refused before it leaves obid. Every call's arguments go through the secret scan first (`crate::scan`, as a push's do, in obid's own `home/tmp`, never the job's folder, which its agent can write): a finding, or a scan that can't run, is refused (`Oby didn't send this to Tetrix: the secret scan found something in it.`) and nothing leaves. Every result is scrubbed like a connection's.
- A session the agent saves (`save_session`'s answer) is listed in the job's `outgoing` as `{"action": "tetrixSession", "repo", "id": "<session id>"}`.
- Each call is recorded in the job's `connectionUses` as connection `tetrix`, secret `oby.tetrix.access`, host Tetrix's (never a value), and counted in the job's `tetrix`.
- The Tetrix plug-in (`agent-mcp`) stays for when Oby isn't signed in: then a Claude Code agent gets the plug-in's server at its URL and signs in with its tool's own login, as before. Signed in, obid's server replaces the plug-in's: the same name, the same tools.
- Reviews another Obi asked for get no Tetrix.

**Context first.** Before a job's agent starts (its first turn, and the first after obid restarted it), obid asks Tetrix with its own login about the job: `search_entities` for the issue or pull request (`<repo>#<n>`, else the job's title) and `repo_sessions` for the repository, 10 seconds at most each, each through the secret scan first (one it doesn't pass isn't asked). What comes back is written to `.obi/context/tetrix.md` with the plug-ins' context (obid only: plug-ins), quoted as data, and the prompt says to start there, to ask Tetrix before reading many files, and to end with `save_session` and a `Tetrix-Session: <id>` trailer on every commit.

**Jobs' `tetrix`** (Job, above) on obid is filled for jobs whose agent had Tetrix through Oby, and stays null otherwise (a plug-in's server signed in by the tool's own login is the tool's business):

```json
{"connected": true, "calls": {"search_entities": 2, "get_entity": 1, "save_session": 1}, "rounds": null,
 "reminded": null, "stamps": {"commits": 2, "stamped": 2, "sessions": ["8f2a…"]}, "author": null,
 "context": true, "saved": ["8f2a…"],
 "gaps": {"noResults": ["acme/shop#12"], "missing": ["repo:acme/legacy"], "unknownFiles": ["src/new.rs"],
          "failed": [{"tool": "code_impact", "error": "Tetrix said: …"}],
          "sent": "2026-10-18T14:40:02Z", "session": "c41d…", "notSent": null}}
```

- `calls`: every call the agent made through obid, by tool, counted by obid itself (it is the server). `rounds` and `reminded` are null on obid. `stamps`: as above, read when the job's last round ends. `context`: whether `.obi/context/tetrix.md` was written. `saved`: the session ids Tetrix's `save_session` answered the agent.
- `gaps`, the report back to Tetrix of where the graph fell short (null until the job ends): `noResults`, searches (`search_entities`, `query_graph`) that found nothing, by their query; `missing`, entities `get_entity` (or another lookup) didn't find, by what was asked; `unknownFiles`, files the job changed that no Tetrix answer named; `failed`, calls that failed, with the error's first 200 characters. At most 20 of each, each at most 200 characters.
- When the job ends with any gap, obid sends them to Tetrix with its own login as a `save_session` of its own (`client` `oby`, `title` `Oby: graph gaps in <job title>`, `repo_hint` the repository, `summary` the counts, `state` `{"kind": "oby.graphGaps/1", "job": "<id>", …the four lists, "agentSessions": […]}`), after the secret scan passes on exactly what goes; a finding, or a scan that can't run, sends nothing (`notSent` says why). `sent` and `session` say when and as which Tetrix session; the send is listed in the job's `outgoing` as `{"action": "tetrixGaps", "repo", "id": "<session id>"}`.
- The summary's last line (not on review jobs) reads as above, followed by `Graph gaps sent to Tetrix: 1 search with no result, 1 file it didn't know.` when there were some.

**Accounts' `tetrix`** (Account, above) on obid is `true` for every Claude Code, Codex and Cursor account while Oby is signed in to Tetrix, else null; `tetrixJobs` and `tetrixUsed` count the account's latest 50 ended jobs with a `tetrix`, and those whose agent called it.

### obid only: the gateway

obid serves the LAN gateway's pairing and forwarding (LAN gateway (#284), above) as the Swift runner does, with these differences (`core/crates/obid/src/gateway.rs`, #446):

- **Where it listens.** Only where the configuration's `gateway` says, an address on `127.0.0.1` like `127.0.0.1:8810`; with no `gateway` there's none. Any other address is refused when obid starts (`gateway is "0.0.0.0:8810": it must be an address on 127.0.0.1 with a port…`), and a busy one stops obid before it serves anything. Once it listens, obid prints a second line on stdout after its first, `obid gateway listening on http://127.0.0.1:8810`. It isn't advertised over Bonjour: phones reach it through the owner's tailnet, with `tailscale serve --bg --https=8443 http://127.0.0.1:8810` (never `tailscale funnel`, which would put it on the internet), and pair at `https://<the Mac's tailnet name>:8443`.
- **Invitations** come from `POST /v1/pairing/invite` on obid's API with its API token, as above. `name` defaults to the machine's name and `hostname` to the `url`'s host and port. A Tailscale hostname is a link's `hostname` as it is, port included (`mac.tail0000.ts.net:8443`).
- **Paired devices** are kept, by their token's SHA-256 digest only, in `home/gateway-devices.json` (0600), not in `devices.json`: a phone paired with the Swift runner pairs again with obid.
- **Forwarding** happens inside obid: an allowed request with a paired device's token goes to obid's own API router with obid's API token and a loopback `Host`, carrying only its method, path, query, body (at most 4 MiB) and `Content-Type`. The answer has passed through the API's own layers, so the vault's secrets are already out; the gateway then nulls `session` and puts `[home]` for the accounts' `configDir` paths and for `home/logins` (where a job's copy of its login lives), as above, and sends only the status, `Content-Type`, `Cache-Control`, and an artifact's `Content-Security-Policy`, `X-Content-Type-Options` and `Referrer-Policy`. An answer that isn't text (not `text/…`, JSON or `…+json`: an artifact's image or PDF) goes byte for byte, as the API's own layer sends it. An answer of known length goes whole; any other (the event stream, `GET /v1/jobs/{id}/wait`) goes chunk by chunk as obid sends it, so a wait's first space reaches the phone at once. A stream ends at its first chunk after its device is revoked.
- **Redeeming** takes only what a phone sends: a request with an `Origin` (a browser) is `403` and one that isn't `application/json` is `415`, before it counts as a try, so a web page can't use an invitation up. A device's name keeps no control characters.
- **The allowlist** is Runner/Routes.swift's, plus `GET /v1/conversations`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /v1/conversations/{id}` (obid only: conversations), `GET /v1/artifacts`, `GET /v1/artifacts/{id}` and `GET /v1/artifacts/{id}/{version}/{path}` at any depth (obid only: artifacts), `GET /v1/jobs/{id}/report`, `GET /v1/jobs/{id}/transcript`, `GET /v1/jobs/{id}/transcript/{n}`, `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/proposals/{index}/file`, and `GET /v1/github/work`, `POST /v1/github/work/refresh` and `POST /v1/github/work/{id}/start` (obid only: GitHub work). Routes obid doesn't serve yet answer `404` through the gateway as they do on loopback.

### obid only: GitHub work

What GitHub wants from the owner, so Oby can show it and do it for them: the open issues assigned to them, the open pull requests waiting for their review (asked of them or of a team they're on), and their own open pull requests. obid reads it with its own `gh` login, as the Lead reads the board: `gh` signs in with its own login (gh's device sign-in from the Mac, or a token the owner gave gh's own store), and obid never reads, holds or passes a token (no file, argument, environment variable, log or event of obid's has one). No webhooks: obid is local and self-hostable, so it asks.

- **How it reads.** One `gh api graphql` query per read, with three searches of up to 100 items each (`is:open is:issue archived:false assignee:@me`, `is:open is:pr archived:false review-requested:@me`, `is:open is:pr archived:false author:@me`, newest update first), each item's boards (`projectItems(first: 5, includeArchived: false)`, with its `Status` field), GitHub's `rateLimit` (`cost`, `remaining`, `resetAt`, read on every read: 9 points with boards, 6 without, measured 2026-10-20) and `viewer`, so the feed is always that of whoever `gh` is signed in as now (after `gh auth switch` too). Every 3 minutes while obid runs, the first right after it starts. A `403` or `429` (GitHub's rate limits, the secondary ones included) waits 3 minutes, then doubles, up to 30 minutes, until a read succeeds; a read that leaves fewer than 50 points waits until `rateLimit.resetAt`. Other failures (no network, a `5xx`) keep the last lists and try again at the next tick. An answer with GitHub `errors` besides its `data` (an organization whose SAML the login isn't authorized for, say; `gh` fails it) still counts: its lists are shown, and `error` says what GitHub left out.
- **Boards need `read:project`.** Reading an item's boards needs gh's login to have the `read:project` scope (or `project`). Without it GitHub fails the whole query, so obid reads again at once without boards: the lists are as always, every `board` is null, `error` is `Board columns need gh's read:project scope: run gh auth refresh -s read:project`, and the log says so once until boards read again. For the next hour a tick reads without boards (one call per read), then tries boards again; a refresh (`POST /v1/github/work/refresh`), or a read as another login, tries them at once, so a refresh right after `gh auth refresh -s read:project` brings them back. Once they read, `error` clears.
- **Not signed in.** When `gh` isn't installed or isn't signed in, the lists are empty and `signedIn` is false: the apps show Connect GitHub. obid reads again at every tick, so signing in shows the work within 3 minutes (or at once with a refresh). The first read after signing in again, or as another login, is a first read: it offers nothing in the inbox.

#### `GET /v1/github/work`

```json
{"signedIn": true, "login": "wellington", "checkedAt": "2026-10-18T09:12:00Z", "nextCheck": "2026-10-18T09:15:00Z", "error": null,
 "counts": {"reviews": 1, "assigned": 140, "mine": 1},
 "reviews": [{"id": "3f9a0c1d2b4e5f60", "list": "reviews", "repo": "obi-ai-bot/obi", "number": 702, "title": "Pair a device from Settings",
              "url": "https://github.com/obi-ai-bot/obi/pull/702", "author": "sangam", "labels": ["area:mac"], "updated": "2026-10-18T08:40:11Z",
              "state": "OPEN", "draft": false, "reviewDecision": "REVIEW_REQUIRED", "checks": "SUCCESS", "board": null, "known": true, "job": null}],
 "assigned": [{"id": "9b1c…", "list": "assigned", "repo": "obi-ai-bot/obi", "number": 640, "title": "…", "url": "https://github.com/obi-ai-bot/obi/issues/640",
               "author": "wellington", "labels": [], "updated": "…", "state": "OPEN", "draft": null, "reviewDecision": null, "checks": null,
               "board": {"project": "Oby — Roadmap", "column": "Todo"}, "known": true, "job": "ab12cd34"}],
 "mine": [ … ]}
```

- `signedIn`: whether `gh` is installed and signed in. `login`: its GitHub login, or null. `checkedAt`: the last read that succeeded, or null (none yet). `nextCheck`: when obid reads next. `error`: why the last read failed, one line with the secrets obid holds taken out (`GitHub's rate limit: reading again at 2026-10-18T09:42:00Z`, `gh isn't signed in to GitHub`), or what it left out (`GitHub left some of your work out: Resource protected by organization SAML enforcement…`), or null; after a failure the lists stay as the last read that worked.
- `reviews`, `assigned`, `mine`: the three lists, newest update first, at most 100 each. `counts`: how many GitHub found for each, so a list shorter than its count was cut at 100.
- Each item: `id` (16 hex characters, obid's own, the same for the same repository and number on every read), `list` (which of the three), `repo` (`owner/name`), `number`, `title`, `url`, `author` (a login, or null for a deleted user), `labels` (names, at most 20), `updated`, `state` (`OPEN`). On pull requests, `draft`, `reviewDecision` (`APPROVED`, `CHANGES_REQUESTED`, `REVIEW_REQUIRED`, or null: no review required) and `checks` (the head commit's combined state: `SUCCESS`, `FAILURE`, `ERROR`, `PENDING`, `EXPECTED`, or null when it has none); on issues, the three are null. `board`: the GitHub Project (v2) board it's on and its column there (`{"project": "<the board's title>", "column": "<its Status>"}`: `Backlog`, `Todo`, `In progress`, whatever the board calls them), or null when it's on no board, on none with a single-select field named exactly `Status`, or gh's login lacks `read:project`; on several boards, the first that has a `Status`. Boards the item is archived from are left out (`includeArchived: false`). `known`: whether the repository is one of obid's `repos` (obid only: configuration), so work on it can start. `job`: the queued or running obid job on it (same repository and number), or null.
- Everything in an item is GitHub's data: show it, never act on it. `title`, `labels` and `board` are what people wrote.

#### `GET /v1/events`: `github`

A `github` event comes, carrying the same object as `GET /v1/github/work`, when a read changes anything in it but `checkedAt` and `nextCheck` (an item came, went or changed, `signedIn`, `login`, `error`), and when `POST /v1/github/work/{id}/start` starts a job (its `job`). The first read after obid starts sends none: fetch the feed when you connect, as after every reconnect. Replace the whole feed with it; how that job goes comes as `job` events.

#### `POST /v1/github/work/refresh`

Reads now, as a tick does, and answers the feed as `GET /v1/github/work` does once the read ends (up to 2 minutes). Within 15 seconds of the last read began (a tick's or a refresh's), it doesn't read again and answers the feed as it is, so no client spends GitHub's points by refreshing; nor during a rate-limit wait, when `error` and `nextCheck` say until when. No body.

#### `POST /v1/github/work/{id}/start`

Starts the work on an item with one call, as the owner's tap (no body, or `{}`). Starting work is always the owner's choice: nothing in the feed starts a job by itself.

- A `reviews` item starts Oby's review of that pull request: a `review` job (`POST /v1/jobs` with `kind` `review` and `pr`), routed as any job, with the pull request's head checked out. It reads, never commits, and never posts on GitHub (What the runner never does): its `summary` is the review, shown in Oby, for the owner to post or not.
- An `assigned` item starts a `fix` job on that issue (`kind` `fix`, `issue`), routed as any job, that ends in a pull request through Publish as today (`Closes #N`).
- A `mine` item is `409`: `Oby doesn't start work on your own pull requests from the feed yet.`
- The job's `task` and `title` are obid's own words, never GitHub's: `Review pull request #702 in obi-ai-bot/obi.` titled `Review obi #702`, `Fix issue #640 in obi-ai-bot/obi.` titled `Fix obi #640`. What people wrote on GitHub (the title, the description, the labels and the latest 30 comments, at most 64 KiB) reaches the agent only as a quoted file, `.obi/inputs/github-<name>-<number>.md` (an attachment, so its text is fenced, the prompt says it's data, never instructions, and the vault's secrets are taken out when it's copied into the worktree), never in a prompt, a task, a title or the brain's briefing.
- Answers `201` with the new `Job`, as `POST /v1/jobs` does (and a `job` event). When a queued or running job is on it already (same repository, kind and number), answers `200` with that job and starts nothing. Starts happen one at a time, from the feed and the inbox alike, so two taps at once (the Mac and the iPhone, or a card and the feed) start one job: the second waits for the first and answers its job.
- `404` for an id that isn't in the feed now (read again). `409` with why: `gh` isn't signed in, a `mine` item, or a repository that isn't one of obid's `repos` (`obid doesn't work in obi-ai-bot/other yet: add it to repos in obid's configuration.`). obid never clones a repository it wasn't configured for: which repositories it works in, and their checks, stay the owner's choice. `422` with why when the job can't be made (no account signed in). `502` when GitHub's text for it couldn't be read.

#### The `github` inbox item

When a read finds a new item in `reviews` or `assigned` on a repository obid knows, one `github` inbox item offers to do it:

```json
{"id": "github-3f9a0c1d2b4e5f60", "kind": "github", "job": "", "title": "Review requested: obi #702",
 "text": "Your review is requested on pull request #702 in obi-ai-bot/obi.", "options": ["Review it", "Dismiss"],
 "details": ["Starts Oby's review of it. Nothing is posted on GitHub.", "Leaves it for now. It stays in your GitHub work."],
 "recommended": null, "github": {"id": "3f9a0c1d2b4e5f60", "list": "reviews", "repo": "obi-ai-bot/obi", "number": 702, "url": "https://github.com/obi-ai-bot/obi/pull/702", "quoted": {"title": "Pair a device from Settings"}}, …}
```

- An assigned issue's item is titled `Assigned to you: obi #640`, says `Issue #640 in obi-ai-bot/obi is assigned to you.` and offers `Fix it` (`Starts a fix job on it, which opens a pull request when its check passes.`) and `Dismiss`. `title` and `text` are obid's own words; GitHub's title is quoted under `github.quoted`, for the card only.
- `Review it` or `Fix it` starts the work as `POST /v1/github/work/{id}/start` does, and the item is done, its `job` the job's id (or the job already on it). The answer claims the item first, so a second answer meanwhile is `409` (`That item is done already.`) and nothing settles it by itself under way; when it can't start, the answer is the same error and the item is open again. `Dismiss`, done or later settle or put it off; nothing is sent to GitHub.
- One per item, ever: its `id` is `github-` and the item's `id`, so an item that's done never comes back, across restarts too. It's done by itself, with no `answer`, once its item leaves the feed (reviewed, merged, closed or unassigned).
- No flood: the first read with no earlier read on record (a new obid, or one that never read GitHub) offers nothing, it only notes what's there; after that, at most 5 items per read, the newest first. Items on repositories obid doesn't know and `mine` items get no inbox item: they're in the feed only.

#### Who reaches it

- On loopback, with obid's API token, like every endpoint.
- Through the gateway, a paired phone can `GET /v1/github/work`, `POST /v1/github/work/refresh` and `POST /v1/github/work/{id}/start` (obid only: the gateway). The list is read-only, and a start does no more than `POST /v1/jobs`, already on the allowlist, would, with obid's own task. The phone gets the feed scrubbed as any answer is.
- Another Obi never does: nothing in Obi-to-Obi exchanges reads the feed or starts work from it, and its typed actions are unchanged.

### obid only: the vault

A connection receives a credential from the same checked snapshot already learned by obid's
redactors. If Keychain Access changes an item between checks, the broker never gets a newer
credential from an unchecked second read. An unreadable store still refuses the request.


The owner's secrets for connections they make (#457), kept and used as the Swift runner keeps them (Engine/Vault.swift):

- **Where.** On a Mac whose `obid` runs in the Mac app's runner home (`~/Library/Application Support/Oby/Runner`, [The Mac app on the same Mac](#obid-only-configuration)), the Swift runner's own Keychain items: generic passwords in the owner's login Keychain under the service `com.deskree.oby.runner.vault`, the secret's name as the account, labelled `Oby vault: <name>`, so secrets the owner added to the Swift runner carry over. Anywhere else, and on Linux, `home/vault/secrets.json` (`{"secrets": {"<name>": "<value>"}}`) as the Swift runner's file vault keeps it: a folder only `obid`'s user may open (0700), the file readable only by that user (0600), each save a new file written 0600 and renamed into place under a lock (`home/vault/.lock`), so two `obid vault add` runs at once each keep their secret. The configuration's `vault` can say either ([obid only: configuration](#obid-only-configuration)). No items, or no file, is an empty vault, which everything reads.
- **What the Keychain is and isn't.** `obid` finds the login Keychain by its path from the user database, never through `$HOME`, and makes items with the Swift runner's service, account, label and accessibility (`WhenUnlockedThisDeviceOnly`). Measured on a Mac (2026-10-17): another program of the same user, even an unsigned one, reads and updates these items without being asked, and macOS adds it to the item's access list, so against such a program the Keychain holds them no better than the 0600 file does. That holds for the Swift runner's vault too, and is [#577](https://github.com/obi-ai-bot/obi/issues/577). Every new build of `obid` that reads an item is added to its access list again, so Keychain Access shows one entry per build: harmless, but expected. Only the program that made an item may delete it, so `obid vault remove` of a secret the Swift runner added fails, saying to remove it there or in Keychain Access.
- **Putting secrets in.** On the machine, as `obid`'s user: `obid vault --config <path> add NAME` takes the value on stdin (typed without echo at a terminal, with echo back if the command is stopped, or piped in), `obid vault --config <path> list` prints the names, `obid vault --config <path> remove NAME` removes one. A value is never an argument (`add NAME VALUE` is refused with the usage line) or an environment variable, and nothing the command prints holds one. A name is 1 to 64 letters, digits, `.`, `_` or `-`, starting with a letter or digit; a value is 8 bytes to 64 KB, with the whitespace around a paste dropped. A common word or a weak pattern (`postgres`, `changeme`, `00000000`: fewer than 6 different characters, a common word with or without digits after it, or under 50 bits by its length and kinds of characters) is refused, since every push holding it would be refused and every answer would have it taken out. No endpoint takes or returns a value.
- **While obid runs.** `obid` reads the vault at start and again whenever it changed (the file's inode, size or time; each Keychain item's name and modification date), so a secret `obid vault add` puts there is taken out from then on, without a restart. A Keychain item's date has whole seconds only, so the Keychain's values are also read again at least every 2 seconds: a second change in the same second is taken out within that. A removed secret stops being taken out. At start the log says which store the vault is and how many secrets it holds, never a name or a value.
- **Answers.** Every `/v1` answer has the vault's secrets taken out of every string, keys too, plain and in every encoded form `obid` knows (base64, standard or URL-safe, at any offset in a longer run, or wrapped over lines as GNU base64 writes it, also behind a diff's `+` or a quote's `> ` and with the line breaks written as `\n` inside one JSON string; hex in either case; URL-encoded, a form's `+` for spaces too), each as `[vault: <name>]`; a string that still holds one some other way becomes that marker whole. An answer with no secret in it goes as it was, byte for byte. The event stream and `/wait` are scrubbed a frame at a time. When the vault can't be read, a request isn't carried out: nothing reaches an agent, the brain, a plug-in or another Oby (no job is made, no words join the conversation). The exceptions only stop work and send nothing anywhere: `POST /v1/stop-all`, `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/stop`, `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/dismiss`, and `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/permission` denying without words (`message` absent, null or blank); those are carried out, so the owner can always stop work. Either way the answer is then `503` with `{"error": "The vault can't be read, so obid answers nothing until it's fixed: <why>"}`, `<why>` naming the file, folder or Keychain service, never a value, and what to do (`run chmod 600 on it`, `move it aside, then add the secrets again with obid vault …`, `unlock the login Keychain`), and an open event stream ends. A check's bundle (`application/octet-stream`) and the web app's files under `/app/` go as they are.
- **Agents.** Every prompt `obid` hands an agent's turn (a job's task, the owner's words for it, a publish-fix round, an Obi-to-Obi review) has the vault's secrets taken out the same way, and so do the files an Obi-to-Obi review hands its agent (the request and the diff in `.obi/inputs/`). When the vault can't be read, the agent gets nothing: the job fails with `The vault can't be read, so obid gave the agent nothing: <why> Once it's fixed, tell the job to go on.`
- **What goes out.** The publish gate and every scan (`crate::scan`: publishing, Obi-to-Obi replies and review results) look for the vault's secrets in those same forms, in every file a push sends, read whole (base64 wrapped over lines joined), and in each commit message; when the vault can't be read, the scan can't run and nothing goes out (`publishError` `Not published: the secret scan couldn't run (the vault can't be read: …).`).
- **The log.** The log layer takes the vault's secrets out of plug-ins' lines with the other secrets `obid` holds, as `[redacted: vault <name>]`; when the vault can't be read, the values it read last are still taken out.

### obid only: conversations

Many conversations with Oby instead of one thread (#493; decided 2026-10-16, D2): each thing asked on Home can start its own, and every device sees them all. The Swift runner keeps its one thread.

A conversation, as `GET /v1/conversations` lists it and the `conversation` event sends it:

```json
{"id": "5b0e2c4d-…", "title": "Review oby PR #33", "pinned": false,
 "created": "2026-10-16T09:12:00Z", "lastActivity": "2026-10-16T09:14:07Z", "cursor": "0-42",
 "tasks": [{"id": "t1", "title": "Fix the login test", "status": "in_progress", "job": "ab12cd34", "note": null}]}
```

- `tasks`: the task list the conversation's agent keeps for itself (`oby_tasks`, obid only: the conversation manages its own work), in its order. Always a list, empty when it keeps none. A change sends the `conversation` event.

- `id`: a lower-case UUID. `title`: the first line the user said in it, as Sangam's design names conversations (`design/app/src/renderer/src/chats.ts`): at most 48 characters (the first 45 and `…` when longer), its first letter upper-case, any other control character a space. A rename replaces it. `created`: when it started. `lastActivity`: its latest turn's `at`, or `created`. Renaming or pinning isn't activity.
- `cursor`: its place in the list right now (pinned or not, and how recent its latest turn is), for paging (below). It changes with every turn and every pin. Treat it as opaque.
- Each keeps its last 500 turns, the oldest pruned, in a private file of its own (`home/conversations/<id>.json`, 0600).
- Every turn names its conversation: `GET /v1/conversation` and the `turn` event carry `"conversation": "<id>"`.
- **At most 200 are kept.** When a new one starts past 200 (and when obid starts with more), obid forgets the oldest that isn't pinned (the one whose latest turn is oldest), as `DELETE` would, with its `conversation` event. Pinned conversations and the latest one are never forgotten, so pinning more than 200 keeps them all. The jobs a forgotten conversation started stay.

**`GET /v1/conversations?after=&limit=`**: the conversations, the pinned ones first, then the others, each newest first (by their latest turn; obid orders turns exactly, not only to the second). `limit` is as for turns: 50 unless asked, 1 to 500. For the next page, send the `cursor` of the last conversation you show as `after`: the list goes on from that place. A cursor is a place, not a conversation: a conversation that stayed where it was is listed once, never skipped. One that moved since (a turn or a pin moves it up, an unpin down) can be missed or listed again; its `conversation` event already told you where it is, so keep one per `id`. `422` for an `after` that isn't a cursor. Fewer than `limit` means there are no more; `[]` before anything was said.

**`GET /v1/conversation?id=&before=&limit=`**: one page of the conversation `id`'s turns, as above; `404` for an `id` that isn't there. Without `id`, a `before` turn names the conversation (the one it's in); with neither, it's the latest conversation (the one with the latest turn), `[]` when there's none. So an app from before #493 shows the latest conversation and pages back through the one it shows.

**`GET /v1/conversations?q=watch target`** finds conversations by what was said in them (#593): a plain text match, no model. `q` is split on spaces, each word lower-cased with the punctuation around it taken off (`target?` is `target`, `off-limits` stays whole), and a conversation matches when its title, or one of its turns, holds every word, in any case. The list is in the same order as without `q`, with `after` and `limit` as above, and each conversation has one more key, `turn`: the latest turn that holds the words, `{"id", "who", "at", "text"}`, its `text` cut to 500 characters around the first word (`…` where it's cut), or `null` when only the title holds them. Only the conversations obid keeps are searched, so a deleted one never appears, and neither do turns pruned past the 500 kept. `422` for a `q` with no word in it or longer than 200 characters.

**`PATCH /v1/conversations/{id}`** with `{"title": "Vault questions"}`, `{"pinned": true}` or both renames or pins it, and answers the conversation. The title is trimmed: one line of 1 to 100 characters, with no line break (`\n`, `\r`, U+2028, U+2029) or other control character. `422` for neither key or a title that breaks those rules; `400` for a body that isn't that JSON (another key, a `title` that isn't a string, a `pinned` that isn't `true` or `false`); `404` for an id that isn't there.

**`DELETE /v1/conversations/{id}`** forgets it and its turns: `204`, `404` for an id that isn't there. The jobs it started stay, and keep its id in their `conversation`: show such a job's card on its own.

**`POST /v1/plans`** takes `conversation`, an `id` (the words join it; `404` if it isn't there), or `"new": true` (they start a new conversation, titled from them); both is `422`. With neither, the words join the latest conversation, or start the first one: the apps from before #493 (the Mac, the iPhone, the Watch) send neither and show one thread, and the Watch and the talk key carry on the latest conversation (#493). A screen that starts a conversation, like Home, sends `"new": true`. The brain reads only that conversation's turns (or the `turns` the app sends). Every answer, whatever its `kind`, carries `"conversation": "<id>"`, the one the words joined. The body may carry `"id"`, a lower-case UUID the app gave the words (anything else is `422`): the user's turn takes it, so the app's own copy of the words gives way to obid's turn of the same id. Words sent again with the id of the user's turn of the same words, where they go (the conversation named, the latest, or with `"new": true` the one those words started; an app's Try again after an answer it lost), add nothing and start nothing: the answer is `{"kind": "none", "repeated": true, "conversation": <that one>}`. An id any other turn has (other words, or another conversation) is `409`.

- A plan obid refuses is never a `422` (#546): obid tells the brain why and lets it try once more, then Codex plans, then keyword rules, which never act on the user's first words (they reply "Oby couldn't think that through: say it again", or ask which repository; to "continue", "go on" or "keep going" they reply "Oby couldn't think that through: say “continue” again in a moment.", never asking which repository, #632). The conversation keeps the words and that answer.
- When no brain answers at all (each one asked timed out or failed, and none sent a plan obid refused), rules don't guess: the reply says which brain and how, and that nothing started, as `"Oby's brain couldn't answer: Claude Code and Codex both took too long, so nothing was started. Try again."` (`Claude Code failed, then Codex took too long`, and so on). When the Mac is very busy it says so instead of "Try again.": `The Mac is very busy (load 512): try again once it calms down.` After a step that did or looked at something, it says that first and ends `Say “continue” for the rest.` (or `… to pick up from there.`). The turn ends `brain_failed`, its record's `by` is `none`, and the conversation keeps the reply like any other.
- New words in a conversation replace the turn under way in it, as `POST /v1/plans/cancel` would: its plan does nothing, and its request answers `none`. A turn in another conversation goes on.
- A conversation deleted while its turn runs stays deleted: the answer still comes, with its `conversation`, and Oby's words aren't kept.

**Jobs remember the conversation that started them.** A job a plan started has `conversation`, the id of the conversation whose words started it, kept when that conversation is deleted, and every `job` event of it carries it, up to the last one (how it ended): show its events in that conversation. It also has `turn`, the id of the user's turn whose words started it, from its very first `job` event, so the apps put its card under those words the moment it starts, before Oby's answer lists it in `jobs`; A follow-up the owner's words start in its parent's own conversation, and a job the Lead starts, have that turn too. `turn` is null for a job no turn started directly (a follow-up told from its page or from another conversation, a Start or Confirm in the inbox, an event turn's). The apps count it only for a job of the conversation on screen. A job from `POST /v1/jobs`, or another Obi's review, has `conversation` null. An inbox item names its `job`, so it belongs where its job does. A routine's runs go to the routine's conversation ([obid only: routines](#obid-only-routines-395)): `conversation` is the routine's, `turn` is null (no user's turn started it, Run now from chat included, which starts on the owner's Confirm), and the turn that says the run started lists its job, so its card goes there. Work that comes in from elsewhere (another Oby, GitHub) doesn't yet start a conversation of its own.

**A job tells its conversation how it ended (#351).** Each time a job a conversation started goes from `queued` or `running` to an end, obid adds one Oby turn to that conversation, with the job's card (`jobs` is `[<id>]`) and `"ended": true`, a key only these turns have. No model writes it: its first line is obid's own words, from the job's fields: `<title> is done: <pull request URL>` when it was published, `<title> is done, but it wasn't published: <why>` when its publish failed, `<title> is done: Pushed, but <why>` when the push went out and only its pull request failed, `<title> is done and pushed.` for a remote with no pull requests (#627), `<title> is done.`, `<title> failed.`, `<title> stalled: <stalled.text>. Its branch is kept and nothing was pushed.` (a stalled job, whose conversation then decides what comes next: obid only: the conversation supervises its jobs), `<title> was stopped.` or `<title> was interrupted.`, the title as `title` reads, cut to 80 characters. When the job's `summary` has a line, its first one (cut to 300 characters) follows on a second line: for a failed job, why it failed (not for a stalled one, whose summary starts with the same stall). Every secret obid holds is taken out of both, as `[redacted: <name>]`. The summary may be its agent's words, so the brain's briefing reads only the first line of such a turn; what the agent wrote stays in the quoted `work.md` (obid only: the brain). A job that ends again after Try again or one more round says so again. A job from `POST /v1/jobs` or another Obi's review (no `conversation`), a dismissed job, and what had ended when obid started add nothing; a conversation deleted meanwhile isn't told. This turn is apart from the event orchestrator's (obid only: the brain), which still reads a failed job and acts in its own conversation, `orchestrator`.

**Events.** A `conversation` event comes with the conversation as listed whenever that changes: when it starts, with each turn (before the `turn` event, so a new conversation is known before its first turn), and when it's renamed or pinned. Replace the conversation with that `id` and sort again. When one is deleted or forgotten, it's `{"id": "<id>", "deleted": true}`: take it out. An app from before #493 adds every `turn` to the one thread it shows, whichever conversation it's in, until it fetches again.

**From one thread to many.** When obid starts, the single thread it kept before #493 (`home/conversation.json`) becomes the first conversation, with all its turns. Its `id` is the thread's first turn's id when obid made that id (a lower-case UUID), and a new one otherwise; its `title` comes from the first thing the user said in it, and its `created` is the first turn's `at`. The conversation is written, the folder's entries are synced to disk, and only then is the old file moved aside to `home/conversation.json.pre-493`, unchanged, for a rollback to an obid from before #493 (rename it back). It isn't read again, and deleting the conversation doesn't delete it: delete it once a rollback is no longer wanted. A move cut short is finished at the next start, never made twice.

**Through the gateway.** On obid's gateway (obid only: the gateway, below), `GET /v1/conversations`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /v1/conversations/{id}` are on the allowlist, so a paired phone lists, renames and deletes conversations as the web app does. The Swift runner's gateway (Runner/Routes.swift) doesn't list them; `GET /v1/conversation` and `POST /v1/plans` are on both.

**On the Mac.** When the runner on this Mac is obid (`GET /v1/conversations` answers), the window's side panel lists the conversations, Pinned above Recent, and searches, renames (`PATCH`), pins (`PATCH`) and deletes (`DELETE`) them. A row opens that conversation (`GET /v1/conversation?id=`) and the box in it sends `conversation`. Home's box sends `"new": true`, and so do the first words of a New conversation, which opens empty. The talk key's words send neither, so they carry on in the latest conversation, unless the window is in front on the conversation page, where they join the one shown. Each conversation shows only its own turns. A job's page, and a task's menu, lead to the conversation named by the job's `conversation`, while that conversation is listed; deleted, the job has none to go to. The Mac follows the `conversation` event, and shows only the `turn` events of the conversation on screen. When the runner answers `404` to `GET /v1/conversations` (the Swift runner), or the runner isn't the one on this Mac (the Mac's own brain plans then), the Mac keeps today's single thread, hides the list and sends neither `conversation` nor `new`.

**Memory isn't shared between conversations yet.** What the user decides in one conversation ("never touch the Watch target") should hold in all of them (#493). obid's brain is briefed on the work (obid only: the brain, below), but the conversation it reads is only that conversation's own turns, and it has no memory that could carry a decision. Its remembered decisions (below) are the owner's answers to other Obis' requests, not what they say to Oby. obid keeps memories now (obid only: memory, below), but the brain doesn't read them yet (the rest of #378): until it does, a decision said in one conversation doesn't reach the others.

### obid only: memory (#378, first part)

What Oby remembers about the owner and each repository (docs/onboarding.md section 5, "Learned workflow: memory"). This part keeps memories, lists and deletes them, and finds what the owner keeps repeating; the brain, the conversation and the jobs' agents don't read memories yet, and nothing merges by a remembered rule yet (both the rest of #378). The Swift runner has none: `404`.

**Where.** One Markdown file per memory, 0600, in 0700 folders: `home/memory/user/<id>.md` (about the owner) and `home/memory/repos/<owner>/<name>/<id>.md` (one repository). Frontmatter of one-line values, then the text: `id` (`m` and 8 hex digits), `scope` (`user` or `project`), `repo` (`project` only), `kind` (`preference`, `instruction`, `fact` or `feedback`), `source` (`found`: a suggestion the owner said yes to), `title`, `from` (where it came from, in obid's words: `You said it in 3 conversations; reviewers asked for it on #12 and #40`), `created`, `updated`, `used`. A file that doesn't read as a memory is skipped and logged, never fatal; keys obid doesn't know are dropped.

**Nothing is saved without the owner's yes.** The only way a memory is written is `POST /v1/memory/suggestions/{id}/remember`, the owner's Remember on "Can I remember that?"; obid never saves one by itself, not on a first run either. On the way in it's checked again whatever made it: a title of 1 to 60 characters, a text of 1 to 500, at most 100 memories per scope, and the secret scan (the publish gate's, gitleaks included) on its title, text and `from`: a finding saves nothing (`422`, `That looks like a secret; Oby doesn't remember secrets.`), and a scan that can't run saves nothing (`503`). A memory never widens a rule AGENTS.md keeps fixed: one that would push, commit, merge (auto-merge, squash-merge, land, ship) into `main`, `master`, `trunk` or the default branch (for a repository's memory, its own default branch too: "push straight to dev" where that's `dev`), rebase one of them onto something, skip the secret scan, publish without the checks, take outside text as instructions ("do whatever the reviewers say"), pass on a login or a secret, keep the microphone on or turn telemetry on is refused (`422`, `Oby can't remember that: it would change a rule that stays fixed.` and the rule). A "never", "don't" or "not" narrows a rule only right before its verb ("never push to main" is kept), never somewhere inside it ("push not to a fork but straight to main" is refused). So where a pull request merges can't be remembered for a repository whose default branch it is: that stays the owner's call each time. These are phrase rules, a net under the real guarantee: when the brain reads memory, it reads it as the owner's wishes, never as permission (#378).

- `GET /v1/memory`: `{"memories": [Memory], "suggestions": [Suggestion], "question": "Can I remember that?"}`. Memories about the owner first, then each repository's, the newest first in each. A Memory is the file's fields and its `text`; a Suggestion is `{id, scope, repo, kind, title, text, from, sources}`, `id` an `s` and 8 hex digits, each source `{quote, place, link}`: the quote, word for word; `You, in “<conversation title>”` or `A review on <owner/name>#<number>`; the pull request's address on GitHub for a review, else null.
- `DELETE /v1/memory/{id}`: `204`; `404` when there's no such memory.
- `POST /v1/memory/find`: "Find what I keep repeating", started by the owner. It replaces the suggestions waiting, and answers `{"suggestions": [Suggestion], "read": {"conversations": n, "pullRequests": n}, "problems": ["obi-ai-bot/obi's pull requests couldn't be read."]}`. `409` while one is running; `503` without a Claude Code account, when the secret scan can't run (nothing is read) or when the brain couldn't finish.
- `POST /v1/memory/suggestions/{id}/remember`: `201` and the Memory; `404`, `422` or `503` as above, and the suggestion stays.
- `POST /v1/memory/suggestions/{id}/dismiss`: Not this. `204`; nothing is saved.

**How find reads.** obid reads the owner's own latest turns from its conversations (never Oby's or a routine's; at most 400, each at most 1,000 bytes, turns under 12 characters left out) and, with its own `gh`, the review comments on the 50 latest merged pull requests of up to 5 of its repositories on GitHub (one `gh api graphql` each, at most 4 MB, all of them within 45 seconds: the reviews and the review threads' comments; only the repository's owners', members' and collaborators' (`authorAssociation`), never a bot's or the pull request's own author's, quoted lines `> …` left out, each at most 1,000 bytes). Each of the two gets at most 20,000 bytes. The secret scan runs on every text first and drops each one it flags, and the text after it; when it can't run, or finds something it can't place in a text, nothing is read. The rest is quoted to the brain in `work.md`, each text in its own nonce fence under a heading of obid's own words (a source id, and `the person, in conversation 3` or `a reviewer, on owner/name#12`), never in a prompt. The call is a side call with the quick model (one attempt, never Codex, at most 60 seconds: the owner waits on it), boxed with Read on that file alone, through the vault like the brain's turn. Fewer than two places to compare: no call, and no suggestions.

**What it keeps.** At most 10 suggestions, each checked by obid, not the model: every quote it gives must be in the text it names, word for word (any case, any spacing), at least 10 characters, and the quotes must come from at least two places that count for its scope: for the owner (`user`), two of their conversations; for a repository (`project`, one of obid's repositories), conversations and that repository's pull requests (another repository's reviews never count), and from two reviewers when only reviews say it. The suggestion's own words, which the model writes, must say only what the texts it cites say: every name, path, address or command in its title and text (a word with `/`, `@`, `:`, `.`, `_`, `-`, a digit or a backtick) is in them, and two thirds of its other words of 4 letters or more start like one of theirs, so a comment in the quoted file can't put "install github:x/helper" into a suggestion with harmless quotes. Then the rules above, the lengths, and not one remembered already. `from` is obid's, from the places it verified.

**Not yet.** Tetrix: accepted memories aren't saved to Tetrix for teammates' agents (Tetrix's forwarding is for the agents' own tool calls). Declined suggestions aren't remembered, so a later find may suggest one again. No `memory` event; memory isn't on the gateway's allowlist, so only the Mac's Settings › Memory shows it.

### obid only: routines (#395)

A routine is a saved request with a schedule: "every weekday at 9, review the open pull requests in obi". On each run obid starts an ordinary job from it, routed, checked, published and reviewed exactly like a job the owner asked for, and the run shows up in the routine's conversation. obid runs as a login item, so routines run with the Mac's lid closed, as long as the Mac is awake; a Mac asleep at a run's time catches up once it wakes (below). The Swift runner has none. Triggers on events ("when CI finishes", #135) come later.

A routine, as `GET /v1/routines` lists it and the `routine` event sends it:

```json
{"id": "a1b2c3d4", "name": "Review open PRs", "repo": "obi-ai-bot/obi", "kind": "review",
 "task": "Review every open pull request in obi-ai-bot/obi and say what each needs.",
 "agent": "auto", "account": "auto", "mode": null, "check": null, "rounds": 1,
 "schedule": {"every": "weekdays", "at": "09:00"}, "zone": "America/Sao_Paulo",
 "when": "Weekdays at 9:00", "enabled": true, "created": "2026-10-19T12:00:00Z", "createdBy": "chat",
 "conversation": "5b0e2c4d-…", "nextRun": "2026-10-20T12:00:00Z", "failuresInARow": 0,
 "lastRun": {"scheduled": "2026-10-19T12:00:00Z", "at": "2026-10-19T12:00:20Z", "outcome": "started",
             "reason": null, "job": "9f08fd10", "catchUp": false, "ended": "done"}}
```

- `id`: 8 lower-case hex characters. `name`: what Home, Settings and the run's job call it, one line of 1 to 60 characters. `repo`, `kind`, `task` (1 to 2,000 characters), `agent`, `account`, `mode`, `check` and `rounds`: the job each run starts, with the rules of `POST /v1/jobs` (an unknown repository, kind, tool, account or mode is `422`, with why). `agent` and `account` are `auto` unless the routine pins them: then routing picks for each run, as for any job. `mode` null is Settings' default at each run.
- `schedule`: one of `{"every": "hourly", "minute": 15}`, `{"every": "daily", "at": "09:00"}`, `{"every": "weekdays", "at": "09:00"}` (Monday to Friday) or `{"every": "weekly", "days": ["mon", "thu"], "at": "08:30"}` (`mon` to `sun`, at least one). `at` is `HH:MM`, 24-hour, in `zone`. So no routine runs more often than once an hour: every run spends an account's window. No cron yet.
- `zone`: the IANA time zone the times are in (`TimeZone.current.identifier`), never the runner's own: `422` for one obid doesn't know. A time the clocks skip (02:30 when they spring forward) runs once, right after the jump; a time they go through twice (when they fall back) runs once, the first time.
- `when`: obid's words for the schedule, to show as they are: `Every hour at :15`, `Every day at 9:00`, `Weekdays at 9:00`, `Mondays and Thursdays at 8:30`.
- `enabled`: false while paused. `nextRun`: when it runs next, or null while paused. `createdBy`: `chat` (only a routine the owner confirmed from chat; a client that sends `chat` gets `app`), `mac` or `iphone` (anything else reads `app`). `conversation`: where its runs show up: the conversation it was asked for in, or one obid starts for it, titled with its name, at its first run. A deleted one is started again at the next run.
- `failuresInARow`: runs in a row whose job ended `failed` (or couldn't start). At 3, obid pauses the routine and says so in its conversation; a run that ends `done` sets it back to 0, a stopped one leaves it.
- `lastRun`: the latest run, or null. Each run: `scheduled`, the time it was due; `at`, when obid acted on it; `outcome`, `started`, `skipped`, `missed` or `failed` (it couldn't start); `reason`, why it was skipped, missed or failed, or null; `job`, the job it started, or null; `catchUp`, true for a run started late because obid wasn't running at its time; `ended`, its job's end (`done`, `failed`, `stopped`, `interrupted`) once it ended, else null. The job's own status and summary say how it went: a green run isn't a finished task.

At most 20 routines. Each keeps its last 50 runs, in a private file of its own (`home/routines/<id>.json`, 0600).

**Running.** obid checks every minute, and once when it starts. A routine is due when its next time after its last one has come.
- **Never twice.** obid saves the time it's acting on before it starts the job, so a restart or a second check in the same minute doesn't run it again. A due run and Run now are started one at a time, from the busy check to the run's record, so they never both start. When the clock goes back past the time it last acted on, that time becomes now: nothing waits for a time that seems done already.
- **Missed.** When more than one time passed while obid wasn't running (the Mac asleep or off), it runs once, for the latest, with `catchUp` true, and records one `missed` run for the others (`reason`: `3 runs were missed while Oby wasn't running.`). Times more than 7 days old aren't caught up: they're one `missed` run, and the routine goes on from now.
- **No overlap.** While the job of its previous run is still queued or running, the run is `skipped`: `The previous run was still going.`
- **Same rules as any job.** The job is made as `POST /v1/jobs` makes one (`Jobs::create`): routing, the weekly reserve, `maxRunning`, the files claim, the permission mode, the checks, the secret scan and the review all apply unchanged. A routine never widens what a job may do, and its stored task can't approve anything during the run. A run that can't start (its repository or account is gone, no account can take it) is `failed`, with obid's reason.
- The job has `request` and `task` the routine's task, `title` its name, `conversation` the routine's, and `routine`: `{"id": "a1b2c3d4", "name": "Review open PRs", "scheduled": "2026-10-19T12:00:00Z", "catchUp": false}` (null on every other job).

**In the conversation.** Each run adds one Oby turn to the routine's conversation, in obid's own words, with `routine`, a key only these turns have: `{"id", "name", "outcome", "scheduled", "catchUp"}`. A started run's turn lists its job (`jobs: [<id>]`), so the apps show its card there, as a task in Home's Routines group: `Review open PRs started its 9:00 run.` (`… its 9:00 run, which it missed while Oby wasn't running.` for a catch-up), `Review open PRs skipped its 9:00 run: the previous run was still going.`, `Review open PRs couldn't start its 9:00 run: <why>`, `Review open PRs missed 3 runs while Oby wasn't running.`, and, after the third failure in a row, `Review open PRs failed 3 times in a row, so it's paused. Turn it back on in Settings › Routines.`. How the job ended then comes as for any job a conversation started (#351). Missed runs older than 7 days, and a run now, say so too.

**In chat.** The conversation's brain has `oby_routines` (lists them, with their ids), `oby_routine` (a new one) and `oby_change_routine` (`pause`, `resume`, `run`, `delete` or `reschedule`, by id).
- `oby_routine` never saves anything. It checks the routine as `POST /v1/routines` would and puts a `confirm` item in the inbox, `Make the routine Review open PRs (Weekdays at 9:00, review obi in Bypass mode, on Claude Code · Personal)`: the job each run starts as obid describes a planned job (kind, repository, mode, tool, account, check), its next 3 times in its zone, and the task, quoted. A Bypass routine's card also says plainly that every run's job never asks the owner anything. Only the owner's Confirm saves it (the confirm action `create_routine`). The brain says the zone the owner is in when it knows it, and obid's own zone otherwise.
- Only `pause` happens on the owner's words, as stopping a job does: it can only make less happen. `resume`, `reschedule`, `delete` and `run` each put a `confirm` item in the inbox (`Resume the routine …`, `Reschedule the routine …`, `Delete the routine …`, `Run the routine … now`, with a Bypass routine's plain line), carried out only on the owner's Confirm (the confirm action `change_routine`, `target` the routine, `payload` `{"action", "schedule", "zone", "mode", "agent", "account", "check"}`). The card keeps the routine's mode, tool, account and check as it named them: when one changed before the Confirm, the Confirm does nothing (`409`, `The routine's mode, tool, account or check changed since Oby asked: ask again.`), and the item closes saying so. Words in a conversation may be an agent's (a job's summary in the turn that says it ended, a job's step), so none of them can resume, reschedule, delete or run a routine by themselves, and none starts a job that never asks. A turn that read what others wrote (the web, the repositories, an attachment) can't even pause one.
- Event turns of the orchestrator (above) have none of these tools: text from GitHub, agents or failures can never make or change a routine.

**`GET /v1/routines`**: every routine, by name. `[]` when there's none.

**`POST /v1/routines`** with a routine's fields (`name`, `repo`, `task`, `schedule`, `zone`; `kind` (left out, `investigate`), `agent`, `account`, `mode`, `check`, `rounds`, `createdBy` and `conversation` optional) answers `201` and the routine. `422` with why: a field that breaks the rules above, an unknown zone, a conversation that isn't there, or 20 routines already. `400` for a body that isn't that JSON.

**`GET /v1/routines/{id}`**: the routine, with `runs`, its runs newest first (up to 50). `404` for an id that isn't there.

**`PATCH /v1/routines/{id}`** with any of `name`, `task`, `schedule`, `zone`, `enabled`, `agent`, `account`, `mode`, `check`, `rounds`: changes them, checked as on `POST`, and answers the routine. `{"enabled": false}` pauses it, `{"enabled": true}` resumes it. Resuming, or a new `schedule` or `zone`, counts from now: what was due meanwhile doesn't run. `422` as for `POST`, `404` for an id that isn't there.

**`DELETE /v1/routines/{id}`**: `204`; `404` for an id that isn't there. The jobs its runs started stay, with their `routine`.

**`POST /v1/routines/{id}/run`**: Run now, paused or not, without changing when it runs next. `201` with `{"routine": …, "job": …Job}`; `409` while its previous run's job is still queued or running; `422` when the job can't start, with why.

**Events.** A `routine` event comes with the routine as listed whenever it changes (made, changed, paused, run, a run's job ended). When one is deleted, it's `{"id": "<id>", "deleted": true}`.

**Through the gateway.** All of these are on obid's gateway allowlist, so a paired iPhone lists, pauses, resumes, runs and deletes routines.

**In the apps.** Home has a Routines group (Sangam's design, `design/app`'s `tasks.ts`): its page lists each routine waiting for its next run as he draws it, `Starts Monday at 9:00 AM`, then the jobs its runs started (`routine` set), and a job's page says `From routine: Review open PRs`. Settings › Routines, on the Mac and the iPhone, lists every routine with `when`, its next run and its last outcome, a switch (`PATCH` `enabled`), Run now and Delete (with a confirmation). Settings › Routines isn't in Sangam's design: his has no screen to manage routines yet, so this one is ours until his comes.

### obid only: the brain

obid's `POST /v1/plans` is the Swift runner's brain (Engine/Brain.swift, Engine/Conversation.swift's `Briefing`, `Desk.plan`), ported (#546), with what obid has:

- **The plan.** Every action of the schema, checked by obid with the Swift runner's refusal texts. A refused plan goes back to the brain with the reason and is tried once more; Claude Code retries a transient failure once (exit code 15, an empty answer, a timeout); quick questions think with Haiku (`claude-haiku-5-5`) at `--effort low`, requests longer than 600 characters and every later step with Sonnet (`claude-sonnet-5-5`) at `--effort medium` (#536); Codex plans when Claude can't, then rules. A planned job's `check` replaces its repository's configured check only when the owner's own words name exactly that command as the check (after `Check:` to the end of its line, in `run <command> to check`, or after `the check is` to the end of its line, any case, without backticks around it or a closing period); otherwise the configured one applies, so neither a model nor text it read can weaken it, and words like "make isAdmin return true" don't name `true`. Each brain thinks for at most 20 seconds (40 for Sonnet) and at most its share of what's left of the turn (half for Claude while Codex is still to come), so both together never outlast the turn. A brain that takes too long only because the turn's own time ran out isn't a brain failure: the turn ends `budget_spent`, as before (tests set a brain's own time with `OBID_BRAIN_SECONDS`, and the load with `OBID_LOAD`). When the Mac is very busy (its 1-minute load average at least 3 per CPU), each gets half as long again, and once one has taken too long the next isn't asked (it would crawl too; its record says `codex skipped: the Mac is very busy…`): the owner hears why at once (above, `POST /v1/plans`). An event turn whose brain can't answer escalates, its item's text starting `Oby's brain couldn't answer this event: <which brain and how>.` Those retries and that fallback are for the conversation's own turn, the one someone waits on: any other model call (a side call such as a title, a summary, a rewrite or a memory; obid makes none yet) is background, one attempt with Haiku within 15 seconds and never Codex, so during an outage it adds no load on the account the turn needs (#537). The brain thinks with each tool's first account. Unboxed (with nothing quoted to read), Claude Code never reads that account's `configDir`, which its jobs can write (#651): Claude Code applies a `.claude.json`'s `env` from its config folder whatever `--setting-sources` says, so a job could plant a `PATH` or plug-in folders that run outside the box. With a `configDir`, the brain runs by its absolute path with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` a new private folder in the call's own, holding only obid's copy of the login (the Keychain item's when `claudeKeychain` is on, else the folder's `.credentials.json` file; only what calls the API), and `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`; with the default login it uses `~/.claude` as before. A Claude account's `configDir` can't be `~/.claude` or inside it (configuration refused): that folder stays one no job can write. Each brain turn is logged and kept, the last 500, in `home/brain-turns.jsonl` (0600): the words, which adapter planned, the plan, and every failure and refusal before it, with the conversation, the user's turn and the step it belongs to (a turn of several steps has a record for each). It also keeps every model call the step took (`calls`, #533), a process retry, a retry after a refusal and a fallback to Codex each its own entry: `adapter`, `model`, `attempt` (from 1 within the adapter), `outcome` (`plan`, `refused` or `failed`), what Claude Code's result reported (`subtype`, `duration_ms`, `num_turns`, `total_cost_usd`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens`), and `wall_ms`, the time obid measured. A number the CLI didn't report is `null`, never 0; Codex's end of turn reports only its subtype. The log line of each turn ends with these numbers.
- **The briefing** is built from obid's own jobs, inbox and accounts. The conversation part is the last 50 turns, each cut to 500 characters. It carries obid's failure records of the last 7 days (obid only: failures, above; #591) under "## Failures this week": how many, counted by trigger (`failed`, `stopped at a limit`, `publish failed`), the rounds their jobs used in all, then one line each, newest first and at most 20 (`<id>: <trigger> · job <job> (<title>) · <repo> · ended <how long> ago · <n> rounds used`), with how many older ones it left out; with none it says `(None in the last 7 days.)`. What each job kept (obid's `what`, where it was, the end of its report and of its check) is quoted in `work.md` under "## Failure <id>", inside the turn's fence, with `Post-mortem: none yet. Oby hasn't studied this failure.` since obid studies none yet (#571). `read_job` on a job with failure records adds the same for its last 3 under "### Failure post-mortem" (`(no failure recorded)` without one). obid has no usage (each account says "no usage reported yet"), no model settings and no weekly reserves (#450): the accounts part opens by naming exactly those three as not on this runner yet, and tells the brain to say so rather than guess, and the settings part says so too. What agents wrote (a job's step and result, a permission's ask) is in the quoted `work.md`; when there is one, the brain may read it with Claude Code's Read and nothing else, with `--max-turns 6`, boxed like a job (it sees its own folder and its account's login, never obid's home or the person's); its Read is allowed on `work.md` alone and denied on the box's copy of the login, its `HOME` and any `.credentials.json` (`--disallowedTools`, #642), so quoted text can't have it read the login into a plan; without one it has no tool at all. Unboxed, it keeps what a job keeps of obid's environment (`USER`, `LOGNAME`, the locale, the proxy and certificate variables), so Claude Code finds its login in the macOS keychain (#564). Boxed, it can't: the sandbox denies the keychain, so boxed it thinks with the first Claude Code account that is signed in (its login file is there, obid only: configuration). When none is and Codex is set up, Claude isn't asked to read at all: the turn goes straight to Codex, and its record's `refused` starts `claude skipped: no Claude Code account has its login in a file…` (#626). When Claude answers without a plan, why it failed keeps what it said (its JSON `result`, at most 300 characters, like `attempt 1: exit code 1: Not logged in · Please run /login, stderr: <empty>`), not only its exit code. The prompt is laid out so the model's server can cache it (#534): the system prompt and the schema are the same on every turn (job ids, inbox items, accounts, models and repositories are plain strings in the schema, checked by obid with the same refusal texts, and the briefing lists them); the briefing goes from what changes least to what changes most (the repositories, the settings, the accounts, the week's failures, the jobs, the inbox, the conversation, then the time), then the user's words, and the turn's canary ends the user message. When the turn's words or its briefing name a configured repository (`owner/name`, or its name alone as a word), the first 4 KB of that repository's `AGENTS.md` (its `CLAUDE.md` when it has none) join `work.md` under "## <repo>'s own instructions (quoted)", read from obid's clone at `origin/<defaultBranch>` as last fetched (#543): at most 3 repositories and 8 KB a turn, the words' first, inside the turn's quoting fence, with the vault's secrets taken out like the rest of the file. A repository with no clone adds nothing, and nothing is cloned or fetched for it. The system prompt lets the brain take a job's `base_branch` and task details from them, and a `check` only for a repository with no configured check: a configured check always wins unless the owner names another (obid only: the brain); obid's checks on the plan are the same.
- **The loop**, its `step` events (which also carry `conversation`, the conversation the turn is in), Stop and cancel are as above. Turns in different conversations run side by side. The turn's budget (110 seconds, or `turnBudget`) bounds the brain's thinking too: no adapter thinks past what's left of it, and a plan that comes after it does nothing (it's kept in `brain-turns.jsonl` as refused, "the plan came after the turn's budget: nothing done"); the answer is what the turn did, then "That's as far as Oby got in one go: say “continue” for the rest." The same line ends a turn whose later step was left a second or less (`leastThink`, for tests), which no brain is asked to think in (`budget_spent`, never `brain_failed`, #615), one whose budget ran out during a read or a lookup, and one that took its eighth action (`most_steps`). **A turn always ends with words in the conversation (#615)**, also when the brain's own words didn't end it: what the turn did, else what it looked at (`Oby searched obi-e2e/fixture-app for “Hello” and read src/greet.js.`, at most four, then `and 2 more lookups`), then what's left: "That's as far as…" on a spent budget or the eighth action, `, then its brain couldn't answer: Claude Code took too long. Say “continue” to pick up from there.` when no brain answered on a later step (after an action: `<what it did> Then Oby's brain couldn't answer: … Say “continue” for the rest.`), `, then stopped.` after a Stop, and `, then kept going over the same ground: say “continue” to pick up from there.` when it asked a second time this turn for a read, a search or lookups it already had (`repeated`; the first time it's told they're in `work.md`). A turn whose brains all failed before it did or read anything says which and how, that nothing started, and what to do (`POST /v1/plans`, above). Only a cancelled turn, and one stopped before it did or looked at anything, answer `none`. The event orchestrator's turns say nothing of what they looked at. Each action ends as an outcome (done, couldn't, or needs the user to say which), and its step's `done` or `failed`, and whether the turn goes on, come from that outcome, never from the words of its line (#538). Each turn ends with a typed reason, which obid logs as `turn <id> ended: <reason>`: `answered`, `asked_user`, `started_jobs`, `could_not`, `needs_which`, `repeated`, `budget_spent`, `stopped`, `cancelled`, `brain_failed` (no brain answered, or rules stood in for a plan obid refused), `most_steps` or `checked` (below). The answer itself is unchanged.
- **The completion check (#539).** Before a turn that did something answers (it started jobs or acted, and ended `answered`, `started_jobs` or `repeated`), obid lists what the user's words name: issue, pull request and epic numbers and repositories, read as the keyword rules read them, and the briefing's jobs by their titles (a repository named "oby" or "obi" isn't counted: it's how people talk to Oby). Anything the turn neither acted on nor mentioned in its answer goes back to the brain once, after what was done: "You haven't handled: #18. Plan it, or reply saying why not." That's one more step at most (it may come after the eighth); if it still isn't handled, the answer ends with "I didn't start #18." ("I didn't act on Vault secrets." for a job). The answer's `jobs` has the jobs of both steps. A turn whose brain failed, that was stopped or cancelled, whose budget ran out or whose action couldn't happen isn't checked, and neither is one that only answered a question. When the extra step goes on to another action or a read, the turn ends there (`checked`).
- **What waits for the owner's own Confirm** (the owner's decision of 2026-10-16). Nothing the brain reads may change a standing decision, so obid's own checks, not the prompt, hold back what persists or widens trust: `answer_permission` with `always` (it saves a rule) and `set_mode` to `bypass` (the job stops asking). The brain's plan becomes a `confirm` inbox item instead (Inbox item, above), and the turn says it waits in the inbox. Only the owner's own answer, `POST /v1/inbox/{id}/answer` with `Confirm` and the API token, carries it out; `Cancel`, `done` or `later` do nothing, and the brain is never offered a `confirm` item to answer. Allow once, no, and the other modes (`ask`, `plan`, and `acceptEdits`, which lets edits through but still asks before every command: the owner's choice) are done in the turn. So is `set_setting`'s `maxRunning` (#552): it's capacity, not trust. A job the brain plans in Bypass mode (a dispatch whose `mode` is `bypass`) doesn't start: it waits as a `confirm` item whose title obid makes from the job (what, the mode, the tool and account, the check), like `Start fix fixture-app (Greeting fix) in Bypass mode, on auto · auto, check node --test`. A short word never throws work away (#631): `dismiss_job` on a job whose commits aren't published waits as a `confirm` item titled with what's kept, like `Dismiss Average helper empty lists (2 commits kept on its branch)`, and the turn says `Dismissing Average helper empty lists waits for your OK: its 2 commits aren't published. Confirm it in your inbox.`; a job dismissed in the turn says what stayed, `Dismissed Notch three screens (2 commits kept on its branch).` (`Dismissed Notch three screens.` with none).
