The briefing marks each job a conversation started, `started in this conversation` or `started in another conversation` (a job from `POST /v1/jobs` is neither), and `oby_jobs` says `thisConversation`; `retry_job` and `dismiss_job` on another conversation's job wait as a `confirm` item too (`Try Vault tests again`, `Dismiss Vault tests`): `Vault tests was started in another conversation, so trying it again waits for your OK: confirm it in your inbox.` The event orchestrator acts on any conversation's jobs. A `confirm` item doesn't expire; one whose target moved on (its permission answered, its job ended) is closed when confirmed, `409` with why, and its `text` says why.
- **Quoted data is fenced.** Each block of `work.md` sits between a line `<<<quoted-<nonce>` and a line `quoted-<nonce>>>`, the nonce random per turn and taken out of the data, so nothing quoted can close its block or fake another.
- **No vault secret reaches the brain** (obid only: the vault). Its prompt, system prompt, schema and `work.md` go through the vault first, every secret in it taken out in every form, as for an agent. When the vault can't be read, no brain runs (nor rules, in its place): the step's reply says `The vault can't be read, so Oby asked its brain nothing: <why> Once it's fixed, say it again.`, also between the steps of a turn already under way.
- **What a plan may do on obid.** Replies, questions and jobs (a job can't be an epic or read other repositories yet: obid refuses `epic` and `reads` rather than drop them, and a dispatch's `complexity` picks nothing on obid, which runs every job at its tool's own effort); `stop_agent`, `set_mode` and `answer_permission`; `retry_job` (obid has no failed-job inbox item: a job that failed, was stopped or was cut off is told "Try again, picking up where you left off", as `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages` would), `dismiss_job`, `answer_inbox` (any open item but a `confirm` one, with one of its options or the user's words; done in the turn only when it's one-off: `Allow`, `Deny` or the user's words on a permission, `Decline` on another Obi's request, `Cancel` on a `start` item, `Dismiss` on a plug-in event. `Always allow`, accepting another Obi's request at all, any "remember", an answer in words to another Obi's question, and `Start` on a `start` item become a `confirm` item, as above) and `set_setting` (only `maxRunning`: obid has no model settings or weekly reserves). Each action says what happened, one line per job, and ends the turn when nothing happened to any of them. What obid can't do yet is answered with why: `move_job` waits for `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/move` (#447); `start_now` (no job waits for a Start when: `start_when` must be empty, #455); `ask_review_again` (obid's brain can't ask for another review yet). A plan that names both an issue and a pull request (#219) starts the issue's work from the repository's base: obid's jobs have no `fromPR` yet.
- **Continue or start fresh (#541).** More work on a job that failed, was stopped or ran out of rounds goes on in that job, whose agent knows the code: a `fix`, `build` or `test` dispatch with the same repository and issue (or pull request) as such a job of the briefing (finished in the last two days, not dismissed) is refused with `issue #12 has job <id>, which failed, was stopped or ran out of rounds; use retry_job on <id> …`, and the brain plans again. When the user's own words say "new job", "from scratch" or "start over", the dispatch starts a new job. A review is always a fresh job.
- **Briefs carry what Oby found (#540).** A dispatch may hand its job `findings`, up to 10 of `{path, lines, note}` (`lines` like `120` or `120-134`, or null; `note` one line of up to 500 characters saying what is there or what was ruled out), and `done`, one line of up to 300 characters saying what must be true when the job is finished. obid refuses a finding whose `path` none of this turn's lookups showed (`path Engine/Keys.swift isn't in what you read`), and the brain plans again. Both reach the job's first prompt as quoted data, under "What Oby found before starting (quoted, may be wrong)", in the same fence as the brain's quoted file (a line `<<<quoted-<nonce>` to a line `quoted-<nonce>>>`, the nonce random per prompt and taken out of the data), never as instructions: they were written from what repositories, GitHub and the web said. They can't change the job's check, permission mode or permissions, which are the job's own fields. They aren't in the API's job. A dispatch with no research has none, as before.
- **Telling a job something (#542).** `message_job` is the user's too, not only the event orchestrator's: "tell the vault job to skip the Watch target" names one job of the briefing, and what reaches its agent is the user's own words, never the brain's restatement, with every vault secret and every secret obid holds taken out (`[redacted: <name>]`). A **running** job hears them at once, as `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages` would: at its next turn, with `notes` and `step` (`You: …`) showing them; the turn's step reads `Passing your message to <job>` and Oby answers `Told <job> what you said.` A job that **ended** (`done`, `failed`, `stopped` or `interrupted`, dismissed, merged or not, its worktree there or not) gets a follow-up at once, as `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages` makes it (the owner's decision of 2026-10-18, as Sangam's design does; it replaced #644's one more round in the job's own worktree): the words are its request, what the first job did is quoted in its first prompt, and the first job stays as it ended. Oby answers `<job> is finished, so I started a follow-up: <its title>. <tool> has it in <repository> on <account>.`, the follow-up's card on the answer (`jobs`). After a lookup this turn (research), the follow-up is only proposed, as research's dispatches are: a `start` item for the owner's Start (`Oby looked into it first. Start …, a follow-up of <job>?`, then `Your words:` and the owner's words), and Oby answers `<job> is finished, and this turn looked something up, so its follow-up waits for your Start in your inbox.` An event turn's words never start one, even when the job ends between obid's check of the plan and the action. A job takes one follow-up at a time (`POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages`, One at a time): while one works, the words go to it and Oby answers `Already on it in <follow-up>, the follow-up of <job>: I told it what you said.`, its card on the answer to open it; no `start` item is made. What the follow-up may do is what any job may: publishing, merging and secrets keep their own gates. Try again stays `retry_job`. `fix_failure` stays the event orchestrator's (below).
- **The Lead (#589).** Vague goals go to the Lead as above (The Lead: vague goals), with the same routing words, skipped list, plan, checks and reply; what differs on obid: the board and Oby's jobs aren't `.oby/context/` files but the Lead's `work.md`, each table in the turn's quoting fence and through the vault like the brain's file, which it reads with Claude Code's Read and nothing else (`--max-turns 6`, boxed like the brain's read), on Sonnet at `--effort medium`, with each tool's first Claude account and no Codex or rules standing in. It doesn't read the repositories' checkouts. Its call thinks for at most 120 seconds and never past the turn's budget; a plan that comes after it starts nothing. When the board has nothing workable, no model is asked: the reply names what was skipped and why (`Nothing to start on the roadmap. Skipped #1 (job 3f2a… is running on it), #4 (blocked).`). An issue in a repository obid doesn't work on is skipped (`obid doesn't work on <repo>`). Its jobs get no account by name (obid picks, as for any job) and no `lead` yet. Each issue is claimed as issue sync does it, with obid's own `gh`: `status:in-progress` and the hidden claim line at the end of its body, after the secret scan, never a comment; obid doesn't move the board's column yet. One turn at a time checks the live jobs and starts its jobs, so two turns at once start one job per issue. Every Lead turn is kept, the last 500, in `home/lead-turns.jsonl` (0600), apart from the brain's: `kind` `lead`, the conversation and turn, the goal, the words, the plan or why there was none, the skipped list, the jobs started, the issues claimed and the model calls.
- **The event orchestrator (#590).** What happens to a job goes to the brain first, as on the Swift runner (#351). obid builds the typed events itself from its own `job`, `check` and `inbox` events, each read as the job was when it was sent: a job that ends `failed` or `interrupted` (`failed`, `interrupted`), one whose account hit its limit (`limit`), a review error (`review_error`), a Mac or Apple check that ends `failed` or `error` (`mac_check_failed`), and an open inbox item that first shows (`inbox`; never a `confirm`, `start`, `permission` or `question` item, which are the owner's to decide already, or the orchestrator's own). What was there when obid started raises nothing, and neither does a dismissed job, nor a stalled job a conversation started (that conversation supervises it). It waits `orchestratorDelay` (30 seconds) for nearby events to come together, lets a user's turn go first, and takes at most 10 turns an hour. The turn runs in the conversation `orchestrator`, with no words of a person: the system prompt is the brain's with `fix_failure` added, and the user message carries a fixed policy and the events, inside the turn's quoting fence. No rules stand in for it. obid checks every plan of an event turn, not the prompt: it must read the event's job (`read_job`) before anything else; it may only take `retry_job`, `message_job` (its own words to the agent, about the error it read; to a job that ended it escalates, since that starts a follow-up and only the owner's own words start one), `answer_inbox` with `Try again`, `One more round`, `Rebase now` or `Wait for the reset` on an open item of the event's job that isn't a permission or a question, `reply_only` or `ignore`; every job it names must be one of the events'; and at most 3 automatic actions per job per UTC day, an action on a job in the same state as at the last one (the same failure again) escalating instead. `start_now`, `move_job`, `fix_failure` and `ask_review_again` are on the allow-list but obid can't do them yet, so they escalate with the action as Oby's recommendation, as does anything else (`ask_user` with Oby's diagnosis and recommended option, a dispatch, a dismiss, a setting). Escalating changes nothing and leaves one open `question` item, `orchestrator-<id>`, titled `Job <id> needs your decision`, whose text is why, with Oby's recommendation, and the events' summaries; no options, it's done when the owner settles it. A user's words in any conversation stop the event turn after the action under way (its turn ends `stopped`), and when it had done nothing its events are considered again on the next round. Its steps are `step` events with `conversation` `orchestrator`, and each step is kept in `brain-turns.jsonl` like the brain's.
- **Reading and looking up.** `read_job` and the research lookups are as above, with what obid keeps: a job's section has its status and step, its report, its pull request (its URL once published, else its number), what obid sent for it (`outgoing`, one line each, ids only: "(nothing went out)" when nothing did, so a push that went out is never read as nothing, #627), its last merge and Apple checks. It doesn't yet read the state of the pull request it follows (#530), and it doesn't read obid's failure records (obid only: failures, above) yet, so those parts say "(no verdict yet)" and "(no failure recorded)". In the briefing, an ended job with no pull request says what went out: `pushed 2 commits, but its pull request failed`, `pushed 2 commits; its remote has no pull requests`, `pushed 2 commits before, then its publish failed`, or `its publish failed: nothing was pushed`; why is the job's to read, never in the prompt. Code lookups use obid's own clone (`home/clones/<owner>/<name>`), fetched once per turn, at `origin/<defaultBranch>` unless the lookup names a ref. A `search_code` whose words no line has as one phrase is tried again with its words of 3 characters or more, the longest first, at most 3, until one is found (#632); its result then starts `No line has “gateway phone route allowlist”; these have “allowlist”:`. The same lookup twice in a turn runs once. A turn's lookups are kept for the conversation's next turn, the last 20 conversations' in memory (a restart forgets them): when that turn's words are "continue", "go on", "keep going" or "carry on", they're quoted again in its `work.md`, the brain is told what words they were for and to look up only what's still missing, the same lookups don't run again, and they don't count against the turn's 12 (#632). `view_issue` and `view_pr` use obid's own `gh` login; `fetch_url` resolves the host, refuses any private, loopback, link-local or Tailscale address, and connects only to the address it checked (`curl --resolve`), every redirect checked again; it reads only an address that's in the user's words or an earlier lookup's result this turn, and refuses any other whole (status `refused`, before it resolves or fetches anything), so a lookup can't carry out to the web, in a query, a path or a host name, what another one read. The address is never echoed into the brain's next request, only quoted in `work.md`. Why a lookup failed is quoted with its result, never in the prompt. Work planned after a lookup waits in a `start` inbox item: its `text` is obid's own words for the job it keeps (`Oby looked into it first. Start fix fixture-app (Greeting fix) in Bypass mode, on auto · auto, check node --test?`), `options` `Start` (recommended) and `Cancel`, `job` empty until `Start` queues it as planned, when the item's `job` names it. Two `Start`s at the same moment start one job: the first claims the item, the other is `409`, and a job that can't start leaves it open again. `Cancel`, `done` or `later` start nothing. obid doesn't yet add an Oby turn with the job's card on `Start`.

### obid only: the conversation harness

Talking to Oby is an agent session, as talking to Claude Code is (docs/conversation-harness.md, step 1: Claude Code). Unless the configuration says `"conversation": {"agent": "plan"}`, every `POST /v1/plans` turn runs in its conversation's Claude Code session; the plan loop (obid only: the brain) answers only when that can't run, and for roadmap work (the Lead). The request and its answer are as above (`reply`, `jobs`, `conversation`); what differs:

- **One session per conversation.** The first turn starts `claude -p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose --include-partial-messages --permission-prompt-tool stdio --permission-mode default --setting-sources "" --strict-mcp-config --restricted --tools <built-ins, no Bash> --max-turns 50 --model <conversation.model> --append-system-prompt <Oby's orientation> --mcp-config <file>`; its session id, from the `init` line, is kept in `home/conversations/<id>/session.json` with the account it's on. After a turn that answered, that Claude Code keeps running and waits on its stdin, and the conversation's next turn is written to it (stream-json input takes one turn after another; `--max-turns` counts each on its own), so it doesn't start again, connect to Oby's tools again or read its session again (docs/fast-replies.md). It waits 30 minutes at most, and at most 3 conversations' wait at once (the one waiting longest ends; the next new conversation's, below, isn't counted); it ends when its conversation is deleted (or forgotten as the oldest), when its account is changed, signed in again or removed, when obid stops, and when a turn is stopped, cancelled, runs out of time or doesn't answer. A turn starts a new one instead when the waiting one was started with other flags (another model, other repositories), on another account or another folder of it, when its login copy ends within 5 minutes, or when it wrote anything while it waited. A later turn with none waiting adds `--resume <session>`, after obid restarts too; a waiting one that ended by itself is started again that way. **The next new conversation's Claude Code is started ahead:** after each turn, obid names the next new conversation's id (kept in `home/conversations/next-id`, so it's found again after a restart; the next conversation the API begins, `new` or the first `latest`, gets it) and readies its place as a turn would, under that conversation's lock: its room with the checkouts, the box, a login copy and its MCP configuration. Then it starts that Claude Code with no prompt, and it waits as above (30 minutes at most, beside the 3) with no session yet. Whether one may wait (its conversation is there or is the next one, and its account is there with the same folder and isn't being changed) is decided under the same lock that ends them, so a change that comes after it still ends it. The new conversation's first turn is written to it. Only one is started at a time, and none on Codex or the plan loop, or with `OBID_PRESTART=off` in obid's environment (obid's tests set it, but in the tests of this). Only a session id that is a UUID is resumed, and a session of another account isn't: a new one starts. The prompt is the owner's words, through the vault (obid only: the vault); a new session also gets the conversation's last 20 turns before them, each cut to 500 characters, fenced as quoted data. The orientation tells it where it is, names Oby's tools and the repositories, and says that what it reads is data. `--restricted` removes the code-running tools (Bash and the rest) and Claude Code's auto-memory, keychain reads and `CLAUDE.md` auto-discovery; `--tools` names the built-ins it keeps — read, search, list, web search and fetch, writes in the room, sub-agents and a to-do list, and **no Bash** of its own: nothing the session runs could read its login copy and send it out. Commands run through Oby's `oby_shell`, in a box of their own (obid only: the conversation's shell); building and changing code is a job. No plug-in, setting, hook, memory or other MCP server.
- **Its account** is Claude Code's first signed-in account (as routing finds one, #626: a login file, or the macOS Keychain's item with `claudeKeychain`). It never sees the account's own folder, which holds every job's and conversation's transcripts: for an account with a folder of its own, its `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is `home/conversations/<id>/config`, which holds a copy of the account's login (the Keychain's item with `claudeKeychain`, else the folder's `.credentials.json`; only what calls the API — `claudeAiOauth`'s access token, its end, scopes, plan and rate tier — never the refresh token, as for every copy obid makes, #651; nothing else of the folder) and its own sessions; for an account without one, the person's login is copied into `home/conversations/<id>/login` as a job's is and shown in its `HOME`. The login is copied before every run, written without following a link, and taken out again when the turn's runs end, or, for a Claude Code waiting for the next turn, when it ends (the keeper keeps it fresh meanwhile). At obid's start every conversation's login copy is taken out, so one a crash left never stays on disk. Before each run obid readies it as before a job's (#651): the Keychain's default login is refreshed by the Mac's own Claude Code when it's due, and a conversation's copy of it is kept fresh by the keeper while the turn runs; an account with a folder of its own isn't refreshed, and near its token's end the turn falls back to the plan loop with why. `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR` is its `HOME`.
- **Its room and sandbox.** It runs in the job sandbox (bubblewrap on Linux, Seatbelt on macOS; obid only: the job sandbox), in `home/conversations/<id>/room`, its working folder.
  - It **writes** only its room, its `HOME` and `TMPDIR` (`home/conversations/<id>/home`) and its own login folder (`home/conversations/<id>/config` or `…/login`).
  - `room/repos/<owner>/<name>` holds a **read-only checkout** of each configured repository: the tree of `refs/remotes/origin/<defaultBranch>` in obid's clone (fetched once when there's no clone yet), extracted with `git archive`. A turn never waits for that when it can help it: obid keeps a spare tree of each repository at that commit in `home/checkouts/<owner>/<name>/spare-<sha>` (made at its start and again after each one is taken), which an empty room takes by a rename; a room whose commit moved keeps its checkout for the turn while the new one is extracted beside it and swapped in by renames, so a read sees one whole tree or the other. Only an empty room with no spare at that commit waits for its tree. `room/repos` stays read-only inside the room (Seatbelt denies writes under it after allowing the room; bubblewrap binds it read-only over the room, so it can't be renamed or removed either). A repository that can't be checked out is left out and logged.
  - It **reads** those, the tools on `PATH` (each tool's `bin`, `lib`, `libexec`, `share`, `etc`, `opt` and `Cellar`, never a whole install prefix that could hold the person's data), Claude Code's own install and the system's own folders, and nothing else of the machine's: it runs confined (`wrap_reading_only_its_own`, as an answer to another person's Obi does), so not obid's home (the database, `api-token`, the vault, the accounts' folders, other conversations, clones, job worktrees), the person's home, `/tmp` and `/private/tmp` (but the empty `/tmp/claude-<uid>` Claude Code opens), `/private/var/folders`, `/Users/Shared` or `/Volumes`. On macOS every path's metadata stays readable, never its contents.
  - It has the **network** (its model's API and the web). On macOS no Unix socket but the DNS resolver's and its own folders', no keychain, preferences, pasteboard, Apple events or LaunchServices, as for a job.
  - No code-host login (`GH_CONFIG_DIR` is empty, as a job's), no vault secret, no `GH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_*` or `CLAUDE_CODE_*` (but `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR`) in its environment.
  - **Nothing planted carries over.** Before every run, and every turn written to a Claude Code waiting for it, obid removes, without following a link, what the agent could have left for the next Claude Code to load: `CLAUDE.md`, `CLAUDE.local.md`, `.claude/` and `.mcp.json` in the room (and any nested `CLAUDE.md`/`CLAUDE.local.md` anywhere under the room but `repos/` and `attachments/`, whose files are the owner's, whatever their names), and `CLAUDE.md`, `CLAUDE.local.md`, `settings.json`, `settings.local.json`, `commands/`, `agents/`, `hooks/`, `skills/`, `output-styles/` and `plugins/` in its `HOME`'s `.claude/`, its `config/` and its login folders (`--restricted` and `--setting-sources ""` already keep settings and auto-discovery out; this is the backstop). The copied `~/.claude.json` keeps only the auth keys (`oauthAccount`, `userID`, the onboarding flags), never the person's projects, history or MCP state.
- **Its permission prompts** are obid's to answer, never the owner's: obid's fixed rules first (obid only: permissions answered without the owner: no push or post, nothing in the background, no file outside the room), then Sandbox mode's. Inside the room, yes, but never a file under a `.git` folder, in any case (`.GIT`), as named or through a link (its settings and hooks name programs git runs); a write under `room/repos`, no (`repos/ holds read-only checkouts …`); web search and fetch, yes; Bash and every other code-running tool of its own, no (they're not in `--tools` either: commands run through Oby's `oby_shell`, obid only: the conversation's shell); secrets, a login or the vault, anything that reaches outside the room, or a tool obid doesn't know stays inside, no, saying why and that a conversation never asks mid-turn. Oby's own tools (`mcp__oby__*`), yes; any other MCP tool, no. Nothing reaches the inbox.
- **Oby's tools**, the MCP server `oby` in its `--mcp-config` (a file in its `HOME`, written without following a link): `POST /v1/agent/mcp/conversation-<id>` with `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, a token made with the run and good only while one of the conversation's turns is under way (a Claude Code waiting for its next turn keeps it, and it's good again only when that turn starts; meanwhile it's admitted only to connect, `initialize`, `ping` and `tools/list`, and a `tools/call` answers a JSON-RPC error, since no turn is under way for it to act for), only for that conversation's endpoint (`401` otherwise; on Linux it's reached at `10.0.2.2`, as the job broker is). It answers MCP's `initialize`, `ping`, `tools/list` and `tools/call` (JSON, not SSE). Each tool is one of the plan loop's actions, checked by `Plan.problems` against the briefing's ids as they are now (with the turn's own word as the canary no plan may repeat) and carried out by the same code, so its rules are the plan loop's: what persists, widens trust or starts in Bypass becomes a `confirm` inbox item only the owner's answer runs.
  - **A turn that read something untrusted** — a web search or fetch, a read of the repositories' checkouts or of the owner's attachments (from any turn), with Claude Code's own tools or Oby's `oby_read`, `oby_search` and `oby_list` (their code is data, not the owner's words; a search or listing with no path, a relative one, or one of the room itself counts, since it reads `repos/` too), a helper (`Task`, which reads as it likes), or `oby_job`, `oby_conversations` or `oby_inbox` (they return agent- and item-written text) — is treated as the plan loop treats research (#374): the jobs `oby_dispatch` plans wait in the inbox as `start` items for the owner's Start (the answer says `I looked into it first. A draft is waiting in your inbox; tap Start when you're ready.`), and the follow-up `oby_tell_job` starts for a job that ended waits the same way, a `start` item for the owner's Start. The mark is written to `session.json` as soon as it's set (and the session's id as soon as its `init` line names it), so a crash mid-turn doesn't lose it and a later resumed turn stays careful even if it reads nothing itself; a new session starts clean. A turn replaced while it waits for the one before it never starts. A turn that used only the owner's words and the room starts its jobs at once. A `start` item's `text` is what the owner reads: obid's own sentence (`Oby looked into it first. Start …?`), then the planned task under `The task Oby planned:`, or, for a follow-up, the owner's words under `Your words:` and the agent's under `What Oby adds for its agent:`, never the quoting fences (they're the agent's prompt's). The brain reads an item's text only quoted, and the briefing's inbox line of a `start` item carries only obid's sentence. Its Start, on a follow-up of a job whose follow-up is working already, tells that one instead (as `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages` does), and the item is done with `job` that follow-up; only while that one is at work with nothing waiting, checked as the words are taken (a permission or plan waiting would take them as its answer): otherwise `409`, naming it, and the item stays open. A follow-up's `start` item whose work merged after it was proposed (the first job's pull request, or a follow-up's) is done by itself, with no options, its `text` `Closed by Oby: <job>'s work merged after this was proposed.` above the owner's words; anything else, a follow-up that ended among them, leaves it open as it was. Checked whenever the inbox is read. One made before obid kept fences out of its text reads without them (`GET /v1/inbox`). A job's `step` shows `You: ` and the owner's words, never what's quoted after them for its agent.
  - One turn calls `oby_dispatch` at most 10 times and `oby_tell_job` at most 10 times (the plan loop's most dispatches in a plan); past that the tool says `Oby starts jobs at most 10 times in one turn: …`.
  - `oby_jobs` `{status?, repo?, limit?, archived?}`: the jobs, newest first (20 unless asked, at most 100): `id`, `title`, `status`, `step`, `repo`, `agent`, `account`, `mode`, `pr`, `published`, `archived`. Archived jobs are left out; `archived: true` lists them instead.
  - `oby_job` `{id}`: the job's section as `read_job` reads it: status and step, report, pull request, Mac checks, failures.
  - `oby_dispatch` `{repo, task, kind?, agent?, account?, mode?, issue?, title?, check?}`: dispatch's one job (`kind` `build` when left out; `mode` `ask`, `acceptEdits`, `plan` or `bypass`, the owner's default when left out; `check`, trimmed, one line of 1 to 200 characters: the check the owner named; when the repository has a configured check, the model's is kept only if the owner's own words name it as the check, else the configured one applies, as on the plan loop), with `conversation` set and the turn's attachments; its card is on the turn (the answer's `jobs`, the `turn` event's `jobs`). `bypass` waits for the owner's Confirm.
  - `oby_tell_job` `{id, words, title?}`: `message_job`. What the job hears is the owner's own words this turn, as the plan loop sends them (#542), then the agent's `words`, between the turn's fences with the line that says they're data, every secret taken out: a running job hears them at its next turn; one that ended gets a follow-up (as `message_job` does), whose `request` is the owner's words alone and whose task is what the job would have heard, its card on the turn. A follow-up counts against the turn's 10 `oby_dispatch` calls, and a turn starts at most one follow-up of a job (`Oby started a follow-up of <job> this turn already: tell that one instead.`). After the web the follow-up is proposed instead, a `start` item (`Oby looked into it first. Start …?`, the owner's words and the agent's under their own lines), and the answer says it waits for the owner's Start. `title` (optional) names the follow-up, cut to 60 characters. While a follow-up of the job works, it hears the words instead (`Already on it in …`), its card on the turn.
  - `oby_stop_job` `{id}`: `stop_agent` for that job.
  - `oby_inbox` `{}`: what waits for the owner: `id`, `kind`, `job`, `title`, `text`, `options`, `ownersOnly` (a confirm).
  - `oby_answer` `{item, answer}`: `answer_inbox` with an option's words or the agent's own: a confirm is refused (`only you can confirm it, in your inbox`); a job's permission may only be refused, and only with a bare `Deny` or `No` and no words (allowing it, or sending the agent words, is the owner's own); what persists or widens trust becomes a confirm item, as for the brain.
  - `oby_conversations` `{query}`: `find_conversation`'s search, never the conversation asking.
  - A refused call answers `isError: true` with why (`Oby refused that: <the plan loop's problems>.`, or the action's own words). What agents or other people wrote (a job's step and report, inbox texts, past conversations) comes between the turn's fences with the line that says it's data. Every answer has the secrets obid knows taken out.
  - `oby_artifact` `{title, path, kind?, artifact?}`: publishes `path`, a file or folder of the room (relative to it), as an artifact of this conversation (`job` none), or a new version of `artifact`, one this conversation made (obid only: artifacts, below): `kind` `html`, `markdown`, `image`, `pdf` or `canvas`, from the shape when left out. It answers `Published artifact <id>, version <n>.`, or the publisher's own one line starting `Not published: ` as an error. The answer of `POST /v1/plans` and Oby's `turn` carry `artifacts: [{id, version}]`, one entry per artifact (a later version the same turn replaces the earlier); the `artifact` event goes out as the artifact is made. Publishing what the agent itself wrote doesn't taint the turn.
  - `oby_read` `{path, offset?, limit?}`, `oby_search` `{pattern, path?, glob?}` and `oby_list` `{path?, glob?}` read the conversation's room, `repos/` included, the same way for every agent (Codex has no other way to read a file). `path` is relative to the room (or absolute inside it); `..`, `~`, and any path that leads out of the room once every link is followed (`config/`, Codex's `CODEX_HOME` with its login copy, `login/`, `home/`, `session.json`, obid's home, the person's) are refused, and so is whatever obid refuses Claude Code's `Read` (a secret's file: `brain/harness.rs` `rule`). `oby_read` gives a text file's lines numbered (`     1\t…`) from `offset` (1 when left out), at most `limit`, 2,000 lines and 256 KB at a time, each line cut at 2,000 characters (and at most 8,000 bytes of one held, the rest skipped), ending `(The file goes on: read on with offset <n>.)` when there's more; a file with a NUL byte in its first 8 KB is refused as not text. `oby_search` matches a Rust `regex` (at most 1,000 characters) against the lines of every text file of at most 1 MB under `path` (the room when left out) that `glob` matches by name or relative path (`*`, `**`, `?`, `{a,b}`), files obid would refuse to read left out: `file:line:text`, each line cut at 300 characters, at most 200 matches. One search reads at most 32 MB in at most 10 seconds (then it says it stopped at its budget), and ends at once with its turn. `oby_list` gives a folder's entries (a folder ends in `/`, a link in `@`, never followed), or with `glob` every file under it that matches, at most 500. Both walk at most 20,000 entries, never through a link and never into `.git`. A file is opened with `O_NOFOLLOW` and only when it's a regular file whose opened path (`F_GETPATH` on macOS, `/proc/self/fd` on Linux) is still in the room, so a link swapped in after the path was resolved isn't followed. Each answer, a refusal too (it names the agent's own path), comes between the turn's fences, and reading taints the turn exactly as Claude Code's `Read`, `Grep` and `LS` of the same path do (`repos/` or `attachments/`, or a folder holding them). Their steps are `read` (with the file as `detail`) and `search` (the pattern, or the listed path).
  - `oby_continue_job`, `oby_archive_job`, `oby_delete_job`, `oby_change_job` and `oby_tasks`: below (obid only: the conversation manages its own work).
- **Events.** `thinking` (`Thinking`, `running`, then `done` or `failed`) when the turn starts and ends. `delta` `{conversation, turn, text}` for its words as Claude Code streams them (`stream_event` `text_delta`), every secret obid knows taken out of the whole text so far: a piece goes out once its word is finished, and what could still be the start of a secret waits for the next piece, so a secret split across pieces never goes out, not even in part; the rest goes out when the run ends. The turn's final text replaces them. A `step` for each tool call, `running` when it's called and `done` or `failed` when its answer comes back: `{turn, conversation, index, action: "tool", text, tool, detail, state, at}`, `tool` one of `read`, `search`, `web_search`, `web_fetch`, `write`, `agent`, `todo`, `oby_*` (else the tool's name in lower case; the `ToolSearch` lookup before an MCP call sends no step), `detail` the file (from the room), the search, the URL, the command's first line, the helper's description, or the job or item, at most 200 characters, `[redacted]` when it looks like a credential, with obid's secrets taken out, and obid's home and the person's home shown as `[oby]` and `~` (as the gateway does for the accounts' folders). Then `reply` with the final words, every secret taken out (as they're kept in the conversation), and Oby's `turn`, with the jobs its tools started and the artifacts they published (`artifacts`, left out when none).
- **Its budget** is `conversation.budget`, 10 minutes unless the configuration says otherwise, for the whole turn (a second run after a failed one has what's left of it), and 50 model turns. Past it the session is ended, and the answer is what it said so far and "That's as far as Oby got in one go: say “continue” for the rest." Stop (`POST /v1/conversation/stop`, or a bare "stop") ends the session at once, with everything it started; the answer is what it said until then. New words in the conversation cancel it, as before.
- **When it can't run.** With no Claude Code account signed in, the plan loop answers, and its reply ends with the line `(Oby's plan loop answered: no Claude Code account is signed in for conversations.)`. A session that fails (it ended without a result or with an error result) is tried once more, a new session when it was resumed; when that fails too, the plan loop answers with `(Oby's plan loop answered: its agent session failed twice.)`. When the room or the sandbox can't be made, the line says why, with obid's home and the person's shown as `[oby]` and `~`. The event orchestrator (#590) always runs on the plan loop.
- **On Codex** (`"conversation": {"agent": "codex"}`, docs/conversation-harness.md, step 4) everything above holds the same way, but the session is `codex app-server` (obid only: Codex) on Codex's first signed-in account, in the same room and sandbox. Its `CODEX_HOME` is the conversation's own, `home/conversations/<id>/config`, with a copy of the account's `auth.json` (from the account's folder, or `~/.codex`) and nothing else of the account's or the person's: no `config.toml`, instructions, skills or MCP servers of theirs. Before every run obid takes out what the agent could have left there, in its `HOME` or in the room for the next Codex to load (`config.toml`, `AGENTS.md`, `AGENTS.override.md`, `skills`, `prompts`, `rules`, `hooks`, `.codex`, `.agents`), and the copied `auth.json` goes when the turn ends.
  - **One thread per conversation**: `thread/start` on the first turn, `thread/resume` with the thread kept in `session.json` on every later one, after obid restarts too. Both say `cwd` (the room), `sandbox: "danger-full-access"` and `approvalPolicy: "untrusted"` (Codex's own sandbox can't run inside obid's, as for a job: obid's is the sandbox, and every file change comes to obid), and `developerInstructions`, the orientation.
  - **No shell, and nothing else it doesn't need.** Under `untrusted` Codex runs what it deems safe reads (`cat`, `ls`, `grep`) without asking, and one could read its login in `CODEX_HOME`. So these features are off (codex-cli 0.162.1, `codex features list`), each `-c features.<name>=false`: `shell_tool`, `shell_snapshot` (it sources the shell's start-up files in its `HOME` at every thread start), `apps` (ChatGPT's connected apps, whose GitHub tools are a code-host login), `plugins`, `multi_agent`, `hooks`, `browser_use`, `browser_use_external`, `browser_use_full_cdp_access`, `computer_use`, `in_app_browser`, `in_app_local_automation`, `view_image`, `image_generation`, `skill_mcp_dependency_install`, `workspace_dependencies`, `daemon_auto_start`, `worktrees`, and what would carry memory or history into a thread: `memories`, `external_agent_memory_import`, `guardian_conversation_history_tools` and `analytics_plan_history` (off by default, pinned off). With `shell_tool` off Codex has no tool that runs a command: `unified_exec` stays `true` whatever it's set to, and is only how the shell runs. Codex takes keys it doesn't know without a word, so this fails closed twice over. Before every run obid runs `codex <the same -c settings> features list` in the run's own box, environment and `CODEX_HOME` (the run's command line with `features list` for `app-server`, so both see the same settings, managed ones included), and runs no conversation unless every one of those reads `false` in what it printed (the plan loop answers: `Codex's features list says <name> is on whatever obid sets …`; the box's own exit status is no sign, since on macOS its first process ends everything with `kill -1`). And in any thread, a helper's too, a command (a `commandExecution` item in any `item/*` notification, or any `item/commandExecution/*`), a hook (`hook/*`), a tool of an MCP server that isn't Oby's (an `mcpToolCall` item; Codex's own `list_mcp_resources`, `list_mcp_resource_templates` and `read_mcp_resource`, server `codex`, reach only Oby's), or anything else that isn't on obid's list ends the turn at once. The list fails closed, so a kind Codex adds later ends the turn too: the items are `agentMessage`, `reasoning`, `plan`, `webSearch`, `fileChange`, `mcpToolCall`, `contextCompaction`, `userMessage` and `sleep` (`item/started`, `item/completed`, and an item's own `item/<kind>/…`), and the other notifications the thread's and the turn's own news, usage, warnings (`codex.rs`, `NOTICES`), `mcpServer/startupStatus/updated` for Oby's server only, and `remoteControl/status/changed` only while it says `disabled` (nothing remote drives it; a real 0.162.1 sends it at start); any other (`imageGeneration`, `collabAgentToolCall`, `subAgentActivity`, `dynamicToolCall`, `process/*`, …) ends it: `turn/interrupt`, then its processes end without a grace; it isn't tried again, its thread is never picked up again, the conversation stays tainted (the mark is kept in `session.json` without a session), and the plan loop answers `Codex ran a command (…), which a conversation never does, so obid stopped it.` (or `ran a hook`, `called <server>'s <tool>`, `used <kind>`, `sent <method>`). Before every run obid also takes the shell's start-up files out of its `HOME` (`.zshenv`, `.zprofile`, `.zshrc`, `.zlogin`, `.zlogout`, `.profile`, every `.bash*`, `.cshrc`, `.tcshrc`, `.login`, `.logout`, `.kshrc`, `.mkshrc`, `.config/fish`, and `.config` itself when it's a link), never through a link. Its code mode (`exec`, which is how it calls MCP tools) stays: with `features.code_mode_host=false` Codex offers no MCP tool at all (checked on 0.162.1: `oby_ping` was gone from its tools and never called), and its JavaScript has no file, process or network access (`require`, `import`, `fetch`, `process` and `Deno` are all missing). A picture it looks at counts as a file it read, for the taint. Without a shell Codex reads files only through Oby's `oby_read`, `oby_search` and `oby_list`, which obid confines to the room.
  - **Oby's tools** come in its arguments, `-c mcp_servers.oby.url="…/v1/agent/mcp/conversation-<id>"` and `-c mcp_servers.oby.bearer_token_env_var="OBY_MCP_TOKEN"`, Codex's streamable HTTP MCP client, with the turn's token only in `OBY_MCP_TOKEN`, kept out of any command's environment (`shell_environment_policy.exclude`) and learned as a secret, so whatever repeats it is redacted; `-c web_search="live"` turns its web search on. Codex asks before every MCP tool call (`mcpServer/elicitation/request` with `codex_approval_kind` `mcp_tool_call`, the tool named only in its words): obid takes the tool those words name when that server was called for it this turn, judges it by the same rule as `mcp__<server>__<tool>` with the arguments Codex will run (`_meta.tool_params`), and answers `accept` or `decline`. Another server's tool (ChatGPT's apps, the person's) is always declined, for a job's Codex too.
  - **Its approvals** are answered by the same rule as Claude Code's prompts: a command (`item/commandExecution/requestApproval`) as `Bash`, so always declined, even in the room; each file a change writes (`item/fileChange/requestApproval`) as `Edit`. A decline tells the turn why (`turn/steer`). Any command item taints the turn, as the web does.
  - **Its login's tokens** (`auth.json`'s access, refresh and id tokens and `OPENAI_API_KEY`) are learned as secrets when obid copies them, so a delta, step or reply that repeats them is redacted. The copy never carries the refresh token (`tokens.refresh_token` is `""`), and a copy whose access token ends within 5 minutes doesn't run ("Codex's login is copied without its refresh token", obid only: the job sandbox); the plan loop answers with that line.
  - **Events**: `delta` for each `item/agentMessage/delta`; a `step` for each tool item (`running` at `item/started`, `done` or `failed` at `item/completed`): a command that only reads, searches or lists is `read`, `search`; any other `bash`; a file change `write`; a web search `web_search`; an MCP call `oby_*`; a helper `agent`. The turn's text is its last agent message.
  - With no Codex account signed in, the plan loop's line says `no Codex account is signed in for conversations`.
- **Deleting a conversation** deletes its folder too: its room, checkouts, session and login.
- **Attachments** (`attachments` of `POST /v1/plans`, checked as above) reach the session: before the turn's run, obid copies each into the room as `attachments/<turn>/<name>` (the user's turn id; the name cleaned to letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-`, with no leading dot and at most 100 characters), as a job's attachments are copied into its inputs: a text file with every secret obid knows taken out, a binary file that holds one left out, and one larger than 25 MB left out, each written without following a link. The prompt names them before the owner's words, between the turn's fences with the line that says they're data: `The owner attached files to these words; they're in your room, named below. Read them (Read shows images and PDFs).`, then `attachments/<turn>/<name>` one per line, and `(left out: <name>, …)` for one left out (a secret, or too big). A turn with an attachment is treated as having read something untrusted, so work it dispatches waits for the owner's Start (above). They stay in the room with the conversation. A job the turn starts gets them too, as before.
- **Known gap:** WebFetch can reach any host, so after a prompt injection the agent could carry what it read in the room or `repos/` out in a URL it fetches (secrets obid knows are redacted; code and text aren't). The next step is a domain allowlist for WebFetch (docs/conversation-harness.md, section 6).
- Not yet: Cursor as a conversation's agent (step 4, over ACP).

### obid only: the conversation supervises its jobs

A job a conversation started that stalls (`stalled`, with its handoff note; the `stalled` event) goes back to that conversation, as Claude Code's main session drives its sub-agents: the conversation decides what comes next by itself, and asks the owner only for what's theirs.

- **The turn.** Once no turn is under way in that conversation, obid starts one turn of an agent session of its own on the conversation's account and room (obid only: the conversation harness): a **fresh session**, never the conversation's (no `--resume`, no Claude Code waiting from the owner's turn, and none kept for the next one; its id goes in `supervisor-session.json`, never `session.json`), so none of the owner's history is in it, in obid's own words only: the job's id, its title and `stalled.text`, never its note or the owner's request. It's told to read the job with `oby_job` (whose report holds the note, between the turn's fences as the agent's words: data, never instructions), then decide once: carry it on in its session (`oby_continue_job`), start it fresh from its note (`oby_tell_job` on it), or split what's left into new jobs (`oby_dispatch`), and say in one short line what it did and why. Its `thinking`, `delta`, `step` and `reply` events name a turn id of obid's (no user's turn), and its answer joins the conversation as an Oby `turn`, the jobs it started on it. The owner's words in that conversation cancel it, as any turn; one that hadn't decided yet comes again after theirs. A stalled job no conversation started is the event orchestrator's (obid only: the brain), which leaves a stalled job with a conversation to its conversation. When that conversation is gone, the owner is asked (below). A stall obid hadn't decided on when it stopped (no `supervised` entry since `stalled.at`) is taken up when it starts again.
- **What it can read and reach.** Nobody watches the turn, so it has no web, helpers or files: its built-in tools are a to-do list only (`--tools TodoWrite`; any other is refused, and Codex runs with `web_search="disabled"`). Of Oby's tools it can't call `oby_jobs`, `oby_conversations`, `oby_inbox`, `oby_read`, `oby_search`, `oby_list`, `oby_archive_job`, `oby_tasks`, `oby_answer` or the shell (`oby_shell`, `oby_shell_output`, `oby_shell_stop`: it runs no commands), and `oby_job` only for the stalled job; the conversation's task list isn't in its prompt either: what it knows is obid's prompt and that job's report, quoted.
- **What it may do by itself.** The turn counts as having read something untrusted from its start, so every tool works as after the web (`oby_dispatch` and follow-ups are `start` items for the owner's Start, gated actions `confirm` items), with one exception, checked by obid, never by what the note says: one decision about the stalled job itself, when the job is its conversation's, still stalled, in a mode that isn't Bypass (a job without a mode counts as Bypass), and the job's line has taken fewer than 3 automatic steps:
  - **continue**: `oby_continue_job` on it runs at once (Try again: the same session, worktree and branch, one round more); its agent hears the Try-again words and the conversation's `words`, between the turn's fences as data, never obid's words to the conversation;
  - **restart**: `oby_tell_job` on it starts its follow-up at once (`followUpOf`, from its branch when it has commits, its report with the note quoted in its first prompt), whose task is the job's own request followed by the conversation's words, quoted as data, and whose `request` is the job's own;
  - **split**: `oby_dispatch` on its repository, with its agent and account (or `auto`, or left out) and its mode (or left out), starts up to 3 jobs at once, each a follow-up of it as above with the part's words quoted. Its title is obid's, `Follow-up: <title> · part <n>`, never one the turn picks (a restart's is `Follow-up: <title>`), since the title is in obid's own words at the next stall. A split's parts follow it up side by side, each on a branch of its own (a job otherwise has one follow-up at work at a time).

  A second decision in the same turn is refused (`This turn decided already (<decision>): one decision a stall.`), and so is a fourth part (`A split starts 3 jobs at most: …`). Anything else (another job, another repository, agent, account or mode, Bypass, stopping, deleting or changing a job, answering the inbox) follows the tool's own rules for a turn that read something untrusted.
- **A safety limit is the owner's.** A job that stalled at its `ceiling` (its hours) or its `budget` (its tokens) gets no turn: obid asks the owner (below, with `That's a safety limit on what it spends, so going on is yours to decide.`), and nothing about it goes ahead by itself in a supervisor turn.
- **Its limit.** At most 3 automatic steps across a job's whole tree: the job it first follows up from and every job that follows that one up, at any depth. A continue or a restart is one step, and a split is one per part it starts, so the parts' own stalls can't multiply the work (a split into 3 parts uses all 3). Each step is kept on the job that stalled.
- **What it decided** is kept on the job as `supervised` (below) and said in the conversation: the turn's line ends `(<n> of 3 automatic steps taken for this job: <decision>.)`.
- `supervised` (obid only, on the job): `[{"at": "2026-10-19T14:05:00Z", "decision": "continue", "jobs": [], "turn": "<turn id>", "count": 1, "of": 3}]`, oldest first, `[]` when there's none: `decision` is `continue`, `restart`, `split` or `asked` (it left the next step to the owner), `jobs` the jobs it started (a split's parts join one entry), `turn` the conversation's turn, `count` the steps along the job's line with this one (for `asked`, those taken before), `of` the limit.

### obid only: the conversation's shell

A conversation runs commands the way Claude Code's Bash does, inside Oby's sandbox and rules (the owner's decisions of 2026-10-19). The Swift runner has none.

- **obid's tools, not the agent's own.** The shell is three tools of Oby's MCP server (the conversation harness, above), the same for Claude Code and Codex: `oby_shell`, `oby_shell_output` and `oby_shell_stop`, shaped like Claude Code's Bash, BashOutput and KillShell. Claude Code's own Bash stays out of `--tools` and Codex's `shell_tool` stays off: a command they start runs in the agent's own box, which reads the session's login copy and has the network for the model's API, and obid can't take either away from it. obid runs each command itself, in a box of its own. Codex waits up to an hour for an answer from Oby's tools (`-c mcp_servers.oby.tool_timeout_sec=3600`; its own default is a minute), so a long command or an ask isn't cut short; the turn's budget is what bounds it.
- **`oby_shell` `{command, timeout?, run_in_background?, description?}`** runs `/bin/bash --noprofile --norc -c <command>` in the room, with no stdin. `timeout` is in milliseconds: 120,000 when left out, at most 600,000. Each command starts in the room with a fresh environment: a `cd` or a variable doesn't carry over to the next one. The answer is how it ended (`Exit status 0.`) and what it wrote (stdout and stderr together, as written): the first 30,000 characters, then `(The output goes on: read the rest with oby_shell_output <id>.)`. It comes between the turn's fences with the line that says it's data, every secret obid knows taken out. A command that ended with another status, or that obid refused or the owner denied, answers `isError: true`.
- **Long commands go to the background, as Claude Code's do.** A command still running at its `timeout` isn't killed: it goes on in the background, and the answer says `Still running after 120 s: it goes on in the background as <id>. Read its output with oby_shell_output, stop it with oby_shell_stop.`, with what it wrote so far. `run_in_background: true` sends it there at once. `oby_shell_output` `{id, wait?}` gives what the command wrote since the last read (at most 30,000 characters at a time) and whether it's running or ended with which status, waiting up to `wait` seconds (0 when left out, at most 600) for it to end. `oby_shell_stop` `{id}` ends it and everything it started. At most 8 of a conversation's commands run at once, and one that writes more than 64 MB is stopped (`obid stopped it: it wrote more than 64 MB.`).
- **Nothing outlives the turn.** Every command, in the background or not, ends when its turn ends (its run ends, Stop, a cancel, the turn's budget, obid stopping), with everything it started: nothing a conversation started runs between its turns, also while its Claude Code waits for the next one. What a command wrote is kept by obid in `home/conversations/<id>/shell/` (never in a box) and removed with the turn; a command that should keep its output writes it to a file in the room. A command that comes after its turn ended never starts (checked under the lock the turn's end takes). Each command's process group is noted beside its output, so when obid starts after it stopped without ending them (a crash), it ends every group whose leader is still one of the shell's boxes (its command line names `oby-shell`), and removes the folder.
- **The box** is the conversation agent's (`wrap_reading_only_its_own`, the conversation harness, above), narrower:
  - It **writes** only the room (never `room/repos`, read-only as for the agent) and the command's own temporary folder, its `HOME` and `TMPDIR` (`home/conversations/<id>/shell/<command id>/`, removed with the turn).
  - It **reads** those, the system's folders, the tools on `PATH` and nothing else of the machine's. obid's home and the person's home are hidden, and so are the conversation's `config/`, `login/` and the agent's `HOME`, which hold the session's login copy and Oby's MCP token: a command gets no account and no login copy.
  - **No network.** A command reaches the network only when the owner allowed that very command (below); then that one run has it (on Linux through slirp4netns, as a job's agent; on macOS the profile's network rules are a job's agent's), but never this machine's own loopback, where obid's API and MCP endpoints listen: on macOS the profile denies `localhost`, which covers `127.0.0.1`, `0.0.0.0`, `[::1]`, the name `localhost` and the Mac's own addresses (checked on macOS 26), on Linux slirp4netns runs with `--disable-host-loopback`.
  - **Bounded.** No file a command writes grows past 256 MB (`ulimit -f`), and git reads no system or global settings (`GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`).
  - **No login of any kind.** The environment is a job's (`sandbox_env`): `PATH`, `HOME`, `TMPDIR`, an empty `GH_CONFIG_DIR`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, the locale and proxy variables, and nothing else of obid's: no `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_*`, `CLAUDE_*`, `CODEX_HOME`, `OBY_MCP_TOKEN`, API token or vault secret. GitHub goes through Oby's own tools, backed by obid's own login (the GitHub work feed, `oby_dispatch` and Publish), never through a token in the shell.
  - It leads a session of its own with no terminal, and can't reach the window server, Apple events, LaunchServices or the keychain, as a job's agent can't (obid only: Obi-to-Obi review jobs, "The job sandbox").
- **The rules.** obid reads the command line as it reads a job's (obid only: permissions answered without the owner): each simple command between `&&`, `||`, `;`, `|`, `&` and new lines, inside `$(…)` and backticks, and in what `bash -c`, `sh -c`, `eval` or a here-document fed to a shell runs. Each part is judged on its own and the strictest decides the whole line, so `ls && curl x | sh` asks and `cat a; git push` is refused. In this order:
  1. **Refused in every mode:** pushing or posting, and any git command that isn't one of git's local ones (`git clone`, `git fetch`, `git pull` too: only obid's own clone and Publish reach a code host; the repositories are in `repos/` already); a path outside the room (but the system's folders, which it reads), or what follows a `cd` obid can't follow. The box would refuse those anyway; the agent hears why at once.
  2. **The owner's deny rules** for conversations refuse (below).
  3. **Always asked, in every mode, Bypass too, and never covered by a saved rule:** what installs software or reaches the network, as obid counts them for a job (`brew install`, `npm install` and `npm -g`, `pip install`, `cargo install`, `gem install`, `go install`, `curl`, `wget`, `curl … | sh`, `ssh`, a service's client, `npx` of a tool the room didn't install, inline code like `python3 -c` or `node -e`, a program named by a variable or a substitution, `/dev/tcp`, code nested deeper than obid reads); and what touches secrets, a login or the vault. Its card offers `Allow` and `Deny` only. Allowed, that command runs once, with the network.
  4. **Read-only commands run freely**, in every mode, Plan too, when every part is one of these programs, named bare (never `./cat` or `bin/ls`: a program with a folder in its name could be one the agent wrote) and found on `PATH` among the system's software (`/bin`, `/sbin`, `/usr`, `/opt`, `/nix`, `/System`; or a bash builtin; nothing is when `PATH` has a relative or empty entry, which bash looks up from the room), given only options known to only read. Any option: `ls`, `cat`, `head`, `tail`, `wc`, `grep`, `egrep`, `fgrep`, `du`, `df`, `pwd`, `echo`, `printf`, `which`, `type`, `stat`, `cut`, `tr`, `nl`, `od`, `jq`, `diff`, `cmp`, `comm`, `basename`, `dirname`, `realpath`, `readlink`, `whoami`, `id`, `uname`, `true`, `false`, `test`, `[`, `shasum`, `sha256sum`, `md5`, `md5sum`, `cksum`, `column`. Only the options obid lists, by name, for `sort` (never `-o`, `-T` or `--compress-program`), `find` (its tests, `-print` and `-ls`; never `-exec`, `-ok`, `-delete` or `-fprint`), `rg` (never `--pre` or `-z`), `tree` (never `-o`) and `file` (never `-C`): bunched letters (`-nr`), an option with its value attached or after it (`-k2`, `-g '*.rs'`), a whole long option (`--glob=*.rs`); anything else, an abbreviated long option too (`--compress=sh`), makes the part one that may write. git is never one of them: the room's own git settings, which a command may write, name programs git runs. And the line writes no file: every `>` goes to `/dev/null` or to another descriptor (`2>&1`, `>&2`), each read whole (`>&1foo` writes the file `1foo`), and no `>(…)`.
  5. **The owner's allow rules** for conversations let a command through when they cover every part of it.
  6. **Anything else** (git too) may write, inside the room only, and follows the conversation's mode: Settings › Permissions' default (a conversation has no mode of its own yet). `sandbox` and `bypass` run it; `acceptEdits` and `ask` ask; `plan` refuses it (`Plan mode: read only, change nothing yet.`). **After the conversation read something untrusted** (the taint, the conversation harness, above: the web, the repositories, an attachment, a job's or the inbox's words, another command's output), it asks in every mode.
- **Every command taints the turn**: what it prints may be the repositories', an attachment's or the web's. So after a command, a later write asks (rule 6), and work the turn dispatches waits for the owner's Start, as after the web.
- **Asking.** Only what the owner reads whole is asked about: a command that would ask and is longer than the 2,000 characters its card shows is refused (`… split it into shorter commands.`). A command that asks waits, and its `oby_shell` call with it, for the owner's answer, as a `permission` inbox item (Inbox item, above): `job` empty, `conversation` the conversation, id `shell-<conversation>-<permission>`, `title` the card's (`Run npm install`), `text` the whole command (at most 2,000 characters, secrets out) and why it asks (`It installs software or reaches the network.`, `It touches secrets or the vault.`, `It may change files in this conversation's room, after the conversation read something untrusted.`, `It may change files in this conversation's room, and the mode is Ask.`). Options `Allow`, `Always allow` and `Deny`; `Always allow` only for a write with a rule obid can make (`Bash(<prefix>:*)`, as a job's card: never a chained command or one that runs whatever follows it). **Only the owner answers it**: the brain's `answer_inbox` and the conversation's own `oby_answer` may only refuse it with a bare `Deny`, and no confirm item stands in. `Allow` runs it once. `Always allow` runs it and saves the rule for `conversations` (`GET /v1/permissions/rules`, `repo` `conversations`), which covers later writes in every conversation, tainted or not; it never covers what installs, reaches the network or touches secrets (rule 3). `Deny` doesn't run it, and the agent hears `The owner said no.`; the owner's own words are a no too, and the agent hears them, quoted. While it waits the turn's budget runs on; when the turn ends first, the item is done with no answer and the command never ran. A restart of obid settles it the same way.
- **Steps.** Each `oby_shell` call is a `step` with `tool` `bash` and `detail` the command's first line, so the apps show `Running <command>` as for any tool step (the apps' folded line counts it in `ran 2 commands`); `oby_shell_output` and `oby_shell_stop` are `bash` steps too, their `detail` the command's id. The step's `done` or `failed` event adds `output`: the last 20 lines (at most 2,000 characters) of what the command wrote, plain text, every secret obid knows taken out, obid's home and the person's shown as `[oby]` and `~`. An app that doesn't show it shows the step as before.
- **Rules for conversations.** `GET /v1/permissions/rules` lists them with `repo` `conversations`, and `DELETE /v1/permissions/rules/{id}` forgets one, as for a repository's. A deny rule (one the owner saved by hand in `permissions.json`) refuses in every conversation.
- **Known gaps:** on macOS a box can read the arguments and environment of the person's other processes (`kern.procargs2`, which Seatbelt doesn't mediate: obid tried `deny process-info*`, `deny sysctl*` and a `kern.procargs2` rule on macOS 26, and none stopped it), so a command could print the token obid gives a job's or a Codex conversation's agent (`OBY_MCP_TOKEN`). obid learns every such token as a secret, so what a command prints reaches the agent and the apps with it redacted, and a command can't use one: it has no network, or with the network no loopback, where those endpoints are. On Linux the box has its own process namespace. Passing those tokens through a file only their process reads is [#777](https://github.com/obi-ai-bot/obi/issues/777). On a Mac whose `git` is Xcode's `/usr/bin/git` (an `xcrun` shim), git can't run in the shell: the box doesn't read the developer folder (a git from Homebrew runs). Matching a command's words is best-effort, as for a job: a script that opens a socket itself isn't caught by the rules, but the box has no network, so it fails. A command the owner allowed once has the whole network for that run (no host allowlist).

### obid only: the conversation manages its own work

The conversation's agent manages Oby's work as Claude Code manages its own (TaskStop, TaskCreate, TaskUpdate, TaskList; Gemini CLI's `write_todos`), with Oby's tools in the `oby` MCP server above. What the owner says ("cancel that", "archive the done ones", "delete the failed job", "switch it to Codex") becomes one of these calls.

**The gate.** These tools act only on the owner's own jobs: a review another Obi asked for is refused (`That's a review another Oby asked for: it isn't yours to change.`). What can't be undone, what widens what a job may do, and anything done on another conversation's job (#631) or in a turn that read something untrusted (the harness's taint, above) becomes a `confirm` item, which only the owner's Confirm carries out; the tool then answers what waits, not an error (`<what> waits for your OK: confirm it in your inbox.`). Its title is obid's own words, never the agent's; words the agent added are in `payload`, and `text` shows them as the owner's words and what Oby adds for its agent, `> ` lines, never the agent's quoting fences. `payload` also holds the job's `snapshot` (Inbox item, above): a Confirm after the job changed is refused. So text that agents or other people wrote can never delete, widen or continue a job by itself. Each call lands in the turn (its answer is part of the reply) and in the job's `changes`.

| Tool | Arguments | At once | Waits for the owner's Confirm |
|---|---|---|---|
| `oby_continue_job` | `{id, words?}` | the conversation's own job, in a clean turn | a tainted turn, another conversation's job, or a job in Bypass mode (`continue_job`) |
| `oby_archive_job` | `{id, archived?}` | always: it's undone by `archived: false` | never |
| `oby_delete_job` | `{id}` | never | always (`delete_job`) |
| `oby_change_job` | `{id, mode?, agent?, account?}` | a narrower mode, or another tool or account, on the conversation's own job in a clean turn | Bypass; any mode that asks less than the job's (Plan, Ask, Accept edits, Sandbox, Bypass, from narrowest); a tainted turn; another conversation's job (`change_job`) |
| `oby_tasks` | `{tasks?}` | always: it's the conversation's own list | never |

- **`oby_continue_job` `{id, words?}`** continues a job that failed, was stopped or needs you (`interrupted`, a stalled one too, with `stalled` set) in its own agent session, worktree and branch, as Try again does (`POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages`): the agent hears Try again's words, then the owner's words this turn and the agent's `words` (at most 2,000 characters in all), quoted as `oby_tell_job`'s are. The job keeps its `id`, gets a new `round`, and `dismissed` and `archived` are cleared. It counts against the turn's 10 `oby_tell_job` calls. Refused: a job that's queued or running (`It's still running: tell it something with oby_tell_job.`), one that's done (`It's done: oby_tell_job starts a follow-up of it.`), one that never started, or one whose follow-up carries its branch on. To start again from a clean session instead, `oby_tell_job` on the job starts a follow-up that's told what the first job did; to split the work, `oby_dispatch`.
- **`oby_archive_job` `{id, archived?}`** archives a job that ended and needs nothing (`archived`, on the Job above), or unarchives it with `archived: false`, as `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/archive` does. A job that needs you is refused (`Dismiss it first, or publish it.`). `oby_jobs` leaves archived jobs out, and lists only them with `archived: true`.
- **`oby_delete_job` `{id}`** always waits for the owner's Confirm (`delete_job`), whose title says what goes: `Delete <title> (its worktree and local branch go)`, or `(its worktree goes; its branch stays, another job uses it)` when the branch is shared, and when it has commits that aren't published, `; <n> commits that aren't published are lost`. Confirmed, it's `DELETE /v1/jobs/{id}`. A job obid would refuse to delete (running, its pull request open, a follow-up carrying its branch on) is refused at once, saying why, with no Confirm.
- **`oby_change_job` `{id, mode?, agent?, account?}`** changes how much the job asks (`mode`: `ask`, `acceptEdits`, `plan`, `sandbox` or `bypass`), as `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/mode` does, and obid's rules for modes stay: a review job stays in Plan. A change to another tool (`agent`: `claude`, `codex` or `cursor`) or account (by id or name; `auto` picks as a new job's does) is for a job that ended: it's added to `moves`, its next round starts a new session on its new choice, which hears the task, that Oby moved it, and what it's told, and a model of the old tool gives way to the new tool's own. A running job is refused (`Stop it first, then change it and continue it.`); so is an account that isn't one of that tool's. Every change is a line in the job's `changes`.
- **`oby_tasks` `{tasks?}`** is the conversation's own task list, kept in its file (`home/conversations/<id>.json`, so a restart, a new session or a summarized one keeps it) and shown in the API (`tasks`, obid only: conversations). With `tasks`, the list is replaced whole, as `write_todos` does: at most 50, each `{id?, title, status, job?, note?}`, `title` one line of 1 to 200 characters, `status` `pending`, `in_progress`, `completed`, `cancelled` or `blocked`, `job` a job's id (1 to 40 characters on one line), `note` at most 500 characters (why it's blocked, say); an `id` left out is given (`t1`, `t2`, …). Without, it answers the list. While the list has a task that isn't `completed` or `cancelled`, every turn's prompt starts with it, quoted as data, so the agent keeps it after its session is summarized. A list written in a turn that read something untrusted keeps that with it (in the conversation's file): every turn it's fed to, a new session's too, is treated as one that read something untrusted, and `oby_tasks` answers it quoted, so text an agent or a web page wrote can't come back as the owner's own plan.

### obid only: Sphere membership

The Obi's place in one Sphere: its key, its owner-signed agent card, its owner's delegation, and the token it exchanges them for (spec `identity.md`). One Sphere per obid in this version.

**`GET /v1/obid/sphere`** answers the membership:

```json
{"state": "joined", "url": "http://127.0.0.1:8787", "sphere": "e2e", "obiId": "obi-wellington", "owner": "wellington",
 "keyId": "ed25519:…", "publicKey": "…", "card": { …the signed agent card… },
 "tokenExpiresAt": "2026-10-14T13:00:05Z", "lastPoll": "2026-10-14T12:00:40Z", "lastError": null}
```

`state` is `none` (no Sphere yet; the other keys null, except `owner`, and `keyId`/`publicKey` once the key exists), `joined` or `revoked` (the Sphere refused its token as revoked). `lastError` is the last thing that went wrong talking to the Sphere, one line, or null.

**`POST /v1/obid/sphere/key`**, no body: makes the Obi's Ed25519 key if there is none, and answers `{"keyId": "ed25519:…", "publicKey": "<base64url>"}`, the same every time after. The private key never appears in an answer, an event, a log or an agent's reach.

**`POST /v1/obid/sphere/card`**: the card the owner is to sign, built by obid around its own key:

```json
{"url": "http://127.0.0.1:8787", "sphere": "e2e", "obiId": "obi-wellington", "name": "Wellington's Obi",
 "ownerKey": "<owner public key, base64url>", "accepts": ["review_request", "question"]}
```

`accepts` names `review_request`, `question` or both: the request types obid takes in this version (anything else is `422`). `description` is optional (one line; else `A personal delegate for an engineer.`). Answers `{"card": { …the agent card without signatures… }}`: `url` is `<url>/v0.1/spheres/<sphere>/members/<obiId>`, `version` obid's, `card_seq` 1 (one more than the current card's when it has one), `issued_at` now, `skills` one per accepted type. The owner signs it as the spec says (identity.md 2.1); obid never holds the owner key. `409` before the key exists, `422` for a field that isn't valid. `url` is the Sphere's base URL with nothing after the host and port: `https://`, or plain `http://` only on this machine (`localhost`, `127.x.x.x`, `[::1]`, as the e2e harness's local Sphere), so the delegation and tokens never cross a network in the clear; the same holds for `POST /v1/obid/sphere/join`. obid follows no redirect from a Sphere.

**`POST /v1/obid/sphere/join`**: joins with an invitation:

```json
{"url": "http://127.0.0.1:8787", "sphere": "e2e",
 "invitation": {"id": "<invitation_id>", "secret": "<secret>"},
 "card": { …the card, signed by the owner… },
 "delegation": "<owner assertion JWT with may_act>"}
```

obid checks the card (identity.md 2.2, checks 1 to 5 and 7), that it carries obid's own `key_id` and `public_key`, `sphere` and `obiId` as given and `owner` its configured owner; and the delegation (signed by the card's `owner_key`, `iss` and `sub` the owner, `aud` `obi-sphere:<sphere>`, `may_act.sub` its `obi_id`, `exp` ahead, `scope` including `receive` and `reply`). Then it redeems the invitation (`POST /v0.1/spheres/{sphere}/invitations/{id}/redeem`), exchanges the delegation and an actor assertion of its own for a token (`POST /v0.1/oauth/token`), reads the directory, and starts following its deliveries (`GET …/deliveries?wait=25`, again as soon as each answers; a new token when less than 60 seconds are left; after an error, again after 1 second, doubling to 30). It keeps the card and the delegation in `home` (0600) and never shows the delegation again. Answers `200` with the membership, `state` `joined`.

`409` when already joined; `422` with why for a card or delegation that fails a check; `502` `{"error": "The Sphere refused it: invitation_invalid: …"}` when the Sphere answers an error (its `code` and `message`, quoted); `504` when the Sphere can't be reached.

### obid only: Obi-to-Obi exchanges

An **exchange** is one request and its replies (spec `protocol.md`, "Exchanges"), as this Obi sees it.

**`POST /v1/obid/sphere/requests`**: the owner's Obi asks another Obi:

```json
{"to": "obi-wellington", "type": "review_request", "body": { …the action body… }, "expiresIn": 3600}
```

`type` is `review_request`, `question` or `handoff`; `body` must match the spec's schema for it (a question is `{"text": …, "about": [refs], "choices": […]}`, `about` and `choices` optional; to ask several Obis, send one question to each: each is its own exchange, answered on its own); `to` must be an active member whose card accepts `type`; `expiresIn` is 60 to 2,592,000 seconds. Every text field of `body` goes through the secret scan before obid signs or sends: a finding, or a scanner that can't run, is `422` and nothing is sent. obid builds the envelope (a new UUID for `id` and `correlation_id`, `created_at` now, `expires_at` now + `expiresIn`, its `key_id`), signs it and sends it (`POST /v0.1/spheres/{sphere}/messages`). Answers `202` with the exchange, `state` `sent`. `422` with why for a body, recipient, expiry or secret scan that isn't valid (nothing sent); `409` when not joined; `502` with the Sphere's code and message when it refuses (`policy_denied`, …), and no exchange is kept.

Only the owner sends requests, through this endpoint (from the apps, or the e2e harness). Agents can't reach it: their tokens answer only `/v1/agent/…`.

**`GET /v1/obid/sphere/exchanges?limit=50`** lists exchanges, the latest first (`limit` 1 to 500). **`GET /v1/obid/sphere/exchanges/{correlationId}`** answers one, `404` for one obid doesn't have:

```json
{"correlationId": "5f0e8c1a-…", "direction": "incoming", "type": "review_request",
 "peer": "obi-ernesto", "peerOwner": "ernesto", "state": "done",
 "decidedBy": "inbox", "rule": null, "inboxItem": "obi-5f0e8c1a-…", "job": "9f08fd10",
 "publishScan": {"ran": true, "clean": true},
 "messages": [
   {"direction": "received", "seq": 1, "at": "2026-10-14T12:00:11Z", "envelope": { …review_request… }},
   {"direction": "sent", "seq": 2, "at": "2026-10-14T12:00:15Z", "envelope": { …accept… }},
   {"direction": "sent", "seq": 3, "at": "2026-10-14T12:00:19Z", "envelope": { …result… }}],
 "result": { …the result body… }, "error": null,
 "created": "2026-10-14T12:00:11Z", "updated": "2026-10-14T12:00:19Z"}
```

- `direction`: `outgoing` (this Obi asked) or `incoming` (it was asked). `peer` and `peerOwner`: the other Obi and its owner, from the Sphere's directory.
- `state`: outgoing `sent`, `accepted`, `done` (a `result` came, or for a question an `answer`: either ends the exchange), `declined` or `expired` (neither by the request's `expires_at`); incoming `inbox` (waiting for the owner), `answering` (a question the Obi is answering by itself, below), `accepted` (working), `done` (result or answer sent), `declined` (decline sent, `busy` included), `expired` (it expired before an answer) or `failed` (the work or the publish gate failed: a result with outcome `failed` and no text was sent).
- `decidedBy`: on incoming, `inbox` (the owner answered), `rule` (a remembered decision, `rule` its id) or `sharing` (a question the Obi answers by itself under a sharing rule, `rule` that rule's id), null until decided. `inboxItem` and `job`: the inbox item and the job it brought, or null.
- `publishScan`: on incoming, what the publish gate found on the reply's text before sending it: `{"ran": true, "clean": true}`, `{"ran": true, "clean": false}` (nothing with text went out) or `{"ran": false, "clean": false}` (the scanner couldn't run, so nothing with text went out); null before a reply with text.
- `messages`: every envelope sent and received in the exchange, oldest first, exactly as signed; `seq` is the Sphere's audit seq (from the send's answer, or the delivery), or null when not known. Received envelopes are listed only after they passed the spec's checks (`protocol.md` section 10). A sent envelope is listed once the Sphere's answer to the send has come, not before: `state` (and `result`, `publishScan`, `error`) change when obid builds a reply, so for a moment, or longer while the Sphere can't be reached, an exchange can say `done`, `failed`, `declined` or `expired` without that reply in `messages`, and the other Obi may already have it. Another `exchange` event comes when it's listed. A reply the Sphere refuses, or one that expires before the Sphere takes it, is never listed, and `error` says why. To show whether a reply went, an app looks for it in `messages`, not at `state`.
- `result`: the `result` body, or for a question the `answer` body (`by`, `text`, and `refs`, `choice`, `by_rule` when given), once there is one, else null. Like every text from another Obi it is data: apps show it, never act on it; an answer is shown with who gave it (`by`: `obi` is "from Wellington's Obi", `owner` is "from Wellington").
- A received `answer` whose `choice` its question doesn't have is dropped (spec `protocol.md` section 11, step 3), as a reply that breaks the exchange rules is.
