Every change sends an `exchange` event on `GET /v1/events`, with the exchange as it is when the event goes out: an older state never follows a newer one ("GET /v1/events", above).

### obid only: Obi-to-Obi requests in the inbox

An incoming request that no remembered decision covers (spec `protocol.md` section 11) waits in the owner's inbox as an item of kind **`obiRequest`**, one per exchange:

```json
{"id": "obi-5f0e8c1a-…", "kind": "obiRequest", "job": "", "title": "Review request from ernesto",
 "text": "ernesto's Obi (obi-ernesto) asks for a review of obi-e2e/fixture-app: feature/average into main at 6d20146. Conditions: tests pass, no blocker findings.",
 "options": ["Accept", "Accept, and remember for this repo", "Decline", "Decline, and remember for this repo"],
 "details": ["Review it this time.", "Review it, and review ernesto's requests on obi-e2e/fixture-app from now on without asking.",
             "Say no this time.", "Say no, and say no to ernesto's requests on obi-e2e/fixture-app from now on without asking."],
 "recommended": null, "created": "2026-10-14T12:00:11Z", "done": null, "later": null, "answer": null, "state": "open",
 "obi": {"correlationId": "5f0e8c1a-…", "from": "obi-ernesto", "fromOwner": "ernesto", "type": "review_request",
         "repo": "obi-e2e/fixture-app", "headSha": "6d20146f366b9fac8907b5066c30ee39aaec09b3", "expiresAt": "2026-10-14T13:00:10Z",
         "acceptance": [{"index": 0, "kind": "tests_pass"}, {"index": 1, "kind": "no_blocker_findings"}],
         "quoted": {"goal": "…", "summary": "…", "acceptance": [{"index": 0, "note": null}, {"index": 1, "note": null}]}}}
```

- `title` and `text` are built from the request's typed fields only (owner, Obi, type, repo, branches, commit, the kinds of its acceptance conditions), never from its text. `text` ends with the conditions by kind, like `Conditions: tests pass, no blocker findings.`; when one is `custom` it adds `1 custom condition, quoted below: obid doesn't judge custom conditions, so the result will say it isn't met.`
- `obi.acceptance` is the request's conditions, typed fields only (`index`, `kind`, and `severity` for `no_findings_at_or_above`).
- `obi.quoted` holds **every** data field of the body, unchanged: for a `review_request`, `goal`, `summary` (null when the body has none) and `acceptance`, one entry per condition in order with its `index` and its `note` (null when it has none), plus `text` for a `custom` condition. For a `question` it is `text` and `choices` (null when it has none): its item is below ("obid only: questions from other Obis"). For a `handoff`, `goal`, `summary` and `acceptance` as above (obid declines a handoff `out_of_scope` in this version). Every app that shows the item shows all of it, as quoted text, never as markup, never to a model as instructions, so the owner sees every condition before answering. `headline`, `happened` and `why` stay null for this kind: no model writes about another Obi's request.
- `POST /v1/inbox/{id}/answer` takes exactly one of the four options (`422` for other words). `Accept` sends `accept` and starts the work; `Accept, and remember for this repo` does the same and keeps a decision (below); `Decline` sends `decline` with reason `owner_declined`; `Decline, and remember for this repo` does the same and keeps a decision. `{"done": true}` without an answer is a `Decline`. `{"later": …}` puts it off; the request's `expires_at` still applies.
- When the request expires first, the item is done by itself with no `answer`, and obid sends `decline` with reason `expired`.
- **The inbox limit** (spec `protocol.md` section 11, "Rate limits"): when the sender's owner (from the directory) has as many `obiRequest` items open in the inbox as `inboxPerOwner` allows (5 unless the owner changed it: "obid only: sharing rules"), a request that would wait there is declined with reason `busy` instead, and nothing reaches the inbox. Requests of every type count.

**Expiry of what obid sends.** A request expires `expiresIn` seconds after it is made (`POST /v1/obid/sphere/requests`). Every reply obid builds (`accept`, `decline`, `status`, `result`, `answer`) has `created_at` now and `expires_at` the later of now + 3,600 seconds and the request's `expires_at`, and never more than now + 2,592,000 seconds (spec `protocol.md` section 3).

### obid only: remembered decisions

What the owner decided once, applied after that without asking ("reviews from ernesto on this repo"):

**`GET /v1/obid/decisions`**, oldest first:

```json
[{"id": "d-3f9a0c1b2e4d", "type": "review_request", "fromOwner": "ernesto", "repo": "obi-e2e/fixture-app",
  "decision": "accept", "created": "2026-10-14T12:00:15Z", "source": {"inboxItem": "obi-5f0e8c1a-…"},
  "uses": 1, "lastUsed": "2026-10-14T12:00:30Z"}]
```

`id` is `d-` and 12 lower-case hex. `repo` is the exact repository, or `*` for every repository (only a question's decline makes one: below). `decision` is `accept` or `decline`. `uses` counts the requests it decided without asking (the answer that made it isn't one), `lastUsed` the latest, or null. A request is covered by a decision with the same `type`, the sender's owner (from the Sphere's directory, never from the message) and its `repo`; an exact repository beats `*`. Only an inbox answer makes a decision; nothing another Obi sends can.

A question has no `repo` (spec `protocol.md` 5.2 and section 11, step 5): its repositories are those its `about` references name, compared ignoring ASCII case, a `job` reference counting only for a job that is obid's own and runs in the repository it names (5.9; else it names nothing obid knows). `Decline, and remember for this repo` on a question keeps a `decline` with `type` `question` for **each** of its repositories, or one for `*` when it is about none. A later question from the same owner is declined by them only when **each** repository it is about has one (its own, or `*`); one about no repository only by a `*` one. `by_rule` names the first repository's. Only a decline applies to a question: what lets obid answer one by itself is a sharing rule (below), never a remembered accept.

**`DELETE /v1/obid/decisions/{id}`** forgets one: `204`, `404` for one obid doesn't have.

### obid only: questions from other Obis

A **question** (spec `protocol.md` 5.2) asks for words: obid answers it, or its owner does, with an **`answer`** (5.8) that ends the exchange. Nothing else ever leaves because of a question, and nothing in one can make obid run, change or publish anything: it never starts a job, pushes, merges, changes a setting, a sharing rule or a decision because of one. Its card must accept `question` (`POST /v1/obid/sphere/card`) or obid declines one `out_of_scope`.

When one arrives (after the checks every request gets, spec section 11, steps 1 to 4):

1. **A remembered decline** that covers it ("obid only: remembered decisions", repository by repository): `decline` with reason `owner_declined` and its `by_rule`.
2. **The same question twice**: one with the same `text` and `about` as a question from the same owner that is still `inbox` or `answering` is declined `busy`.
3. **By itself, under a sharing rule** ("obid only: sharing rules"): the oldest rule for the sender's owner (from the directory, never from the message) that covers each repository the question is about (one about none: a rule for every repository), while that owner and every sender together are under the self-answer limits. The exchange is `answering`, `decidedBy` `sharing`, `rule` the rule's id, and the question counts against both limits there and then, whatever the run ends in (an answer, the owner's inbox, a decline). At most 2 such runs go at once, whoever asked; one waits for its turn. When its turn comes, and again before its answer goes, obid checks that the question is still `answering` and unexpired, that no remembered decline covers it now (the owner may have said "Decline, and remember" meanwhile: then it is declined `owner_declined` with that decision's `by_rule`, and nothing the run wrote goes), and that the rule is still there and still covers it (else nothing the run wrote goes, and the question goes to the owner). obid gives the run what the rule allows, and nothing else (below). It answers `{"by": "obi", "text": …, "refs": […], "choice": …, "by_rule": "<rule id>"}` (`refs` and `choice` only when there are some) once the publish gate passes: a possible secret, or a scanner that can't run, declines it with reason `other` and no text (`publishScan` says which). A model that says it can't answer from the material, that fails, is slow (90 s) or writes something obid refuses, and an obid restart during the run, send the question to the owner instead (step 4).
4. **The owner's inbox** (or `busy`, past the inbox limit): an `obiRequest` item, below. obid sends no `status` while it waits.

**The model run** (spec section 11, "Questions"). On the owner's first Claude Code account (a copy of its `configDir`'s login file without the refresh token, never the folder itself nor the Keychain's login, or a copy of the tool's own login as a job gets, removed when the run ends, and at obid's next start if a run was cut short), in a new folder of its own under `home/answers/` that holds two files and is removed after: `question.json`, `{"quoted", "fromOwner", "question": <the body exactly as received>}`, and `material.json`, `{"quoted", "items": […]}`. The question reaches the model only in that file, never in its prompt or arguments: the system prompt and the prompt are fixed, and say what the files are. It runs `-p --model haiku --tools Read --permission-mode default --restricted --permission-prompts none --strict-mcp-config --setting-sources "" --no-session-persistence --max-turns 4 --output-format json --json-schema <schema> --system-prompt <fixed>`. Two layers keep it to that folder, each holding alone:

- **The system: the job sandbox, confined.** The job sandbox ("obid only: Obi-to-Obi review jobs": writes only to its own folders, nothing of obid's `home` or the person's home, no vault secret, no code-host login, no API token), and on top of it **no file's contents but the system's software and the run's own**: on macOS a Seatbelt rule that denies every read, then allows `/System`, `/usr`, `/bin`, `/sbin`, `/Library/Apple`, `/private/etc`, the time zones and the dynamic linker's caches, `/private/var/select`, `/private/var/run/resolv.conf`, `/dev`, the folder `/private/tmp/claude-<uid>` itself (Claude Code opens it whatever `TMPDIR` says; never what is in it), the tools on `PATH` with their install prefixes, Claude Code's own files, the run's folders and its account (every path's metadata stays readable, so paths resolve); on Linux bubblewrap binds only `/usr`, `/bin`, `/sbin`, `/lib*`, `/etc`, `/opt`, `/nix` and those same paths, never `/`. So `/Users/Shared`, `/Volumes`, `/private/var/folders`, `/tmp`, `/mnt` and every other world-readable folder are out of reach. `the_model_reads_nothing_outside_its_folder` shows it on macOS with a process that obeys the question (no permission prompt to stop it); on Linux `a_run_with_the_network_resolves_a_host_name` runs the confined form and shows it resolves the model API's host and sees nothing of `/run` but the one resolver file bound there.
- **Claude Code.** Read is its only tool, `--restricted` confines its file tools to the working directory, `--permission-prompts none` refuses anything that would ask, and there is no `--allowedTools`. This is the layer that keeps the model out of its account folder (with the sign-in that Claude Code itself must read, so the system lets the process read it) on every platform. Tested by hand with Claude Code 2.1.296 and a model told to read a file outside its folder: refused, `is outside` the working directory. A Claude Code without `--restricted` fails to start the run, and the question goes to the owner. It returns `{"answerable", "text", "refs", "choice"}` (`text` at most 2,000 characters, `refs` indexes into the material, `choice` an index or null). obid cleans the text as `block_text` (CR LF and CR become LF; other control, DEL and bidirectional characters removed; trimmed), keeps only `refs` that name material items (each once) and a `choice` the question has, and checks the answer against the spec's schema. The material is all the model may draw on:

- `jobStatus`: obid's own jobs in the repositories the question is about that the rule covers (all the rule covers, for a question about none), the jobs its `about` names first, then the latest, 20 at most: `{"index", "kind": "job", "ref": {"kind": "job", "repo", "job"}, "title", "stage", "state", "pr"}`. Never a job's logs, diff, prompt, files or agents' words, and never a review another Obi asked for (that is someone else's request).
- `sharedResults`: the results obid already sent that owner's Obis for reviews they asked for, in those repositories, the latest 10: `{"index", "kind": "sharedResult", "ref": {"kind": "repo", "repo"}, "type", "result"}`.
- Never anything about a repository the owner said no to that owner's questions about (a remembered decline for it, "obid only: remembered decisions"), even under a rule for every repository.

**The question's inbox item** is an `obiRequest`, like a review request's, with `title` `Question from <owner>` and `text` built from typed fields only: `ernesto's Obi (obi-ernesto) asks a question about obi-e2e/fixture-app pull request #251, quoted below. Answer it in your own words, or say no.` (`about` in words: a repository, `<repo> pull request #N`, `<repo> issue #N`, `<repo> job <id>` for a job that is obid's own in that repository, while a job reference obid couldn't check is left out of `text` and stays only under `obi.about`; without `about`, `asks a question, quoted below.`):

```json
{"id": "obi-5f0e8c1a-…", "kind": "obiRequest", "job": "", "title": "Question from ernesto", "text": "…",
 "options": ["Decline", "Decline, and remember for this repo"],
 "details": ["Say no this time.", "Say no, and say no to ernesto's questions about obi-e2e/fixture-app from now on without asking."],
 "obi": {"correlationId": "5f0e8c1a-…", "from": "obi-ernesto", "fromOwner": "ernesto", "type": "question",
         "about": [{"kind": "pr", "repo": "obi-e2e/fixture-app", "pr": 251}], "repos": ["obi-e2e/fixture-app"],
         "expiresAt": "2026-10-14T13:00:10Z", "quoted": {"text": "…", "choices": ["Yes", "No"]}}, …}
```

- `obi.about` is the question's references as sent (typed; null when it has none), `obi.repos` the repositories obid counted (above). The question's words and its `choices` are only under `obi.quoted`: choices are data, so they are never `options`; an app may show them as buttons that send their words.
- `POST /v1/inbox/{id}/answer`: `Decline` (or `{"done": true}`) sends `decline` with reason `owner_declined`; `Decline, and remember for this repo` does the same and keeps a decline per repository (`details` says so: for a question about none, `Say no, and say no to every question from ernesto from now on without asking.`). **Any other words are the owner's answer**: 1 to 2,000 characters, sent as `{"by": "owner", "text": <the words>}`, with `choice` when the words are exactly one of the question's choices (a choice that is literally `Decline` or `Decline, and remember for this repo` reads as that button). They must make an answer the spec's schema allows, and they pass the publish gate first: either failing is `422` with why (never repeating a possible secret), nothing is sent, and the item stays open. `{"later": …}` puts it off; the question's `expires_at` still applies.

**What obid sends in a question's exchange.** One `answer` or one `decline`, nothing before it: no `accept` (allowed, never needed) and no `status`.

### obid only: sharing rules

What the owner lets obid tell some people without being asked, and how much each sender's owner can make it do (spec `protocol.md` section 11, "Questions" and "Rate limits"). Only the owner changes them, here; nothing another Obi sends can, and agents can't reach these endpoints. Kept in `home/sharing.json` (0600).

**`GET /v1/obid/sharing`**:

```json
{"rules": [{"id": "s-3f9a0c1b2e4d", "for": ["ernesto"], "repos": ["obi-e2e/fixture-app"], "tells": ["jobStatus"],
            "created": "2026-10-16T12:00:00Z", "uses": 1, "lastUsed": "2026-10-16T12:05:00Z"}],
 "limits": {"selfAnswersPerHour": 30, "selfAnswersPerHourTotal": 60, "inboxPerOwner": 5}}
```

**`POST /v1/obid/sharing/rules`** with `{"for": …, "repos": …, "tells": […]}`, nothing else, adds a rule after the others and answers it, `201`:

- `for`: owners from the Sphere's directory (`["ernesto"]`, 1 to 50), or `"*"` for every member.
- `repos`: repositories (`owner/name`, 1 to 50, compared ignoring ASCII case), or `"*"` for every one.
- `tells`: some of `jobStatus` (the title, stage and state of the owner's jobs, and the pull request each opened) and `sharedResults` (what obid already sent that person's Obis). The spec's third, published pull requests as the code host shows them, needs the code host, so obid doesn't offer it yet.

`id` is `s-` and 12 lower-case hex, a `rule_id` the answer names in `by_rule`; `uses` counts the answers sent under it. `400` for a body that isn't JSON, `422` with why for one that isn't a rule; `409` past 100 rules. A question already being answered under a rule that is deleted or narrowed meanwhile goes to the owner instead. **`DELETE /v1/obid/sharing/rules/{id}`** forgets one: `204`, `404` for one obid doesn't have.

**`PUT /v1/obid/sharing/limits`** with exactly `{"selfAnswersPerHour": 0 to 1,000, "selfAnswersPerHourTotal": 0 to 10,000, "inboxPerOwner": 0 to 100}` answers the limits; `400` for a body that isn't JSON, `422` otherwise. A sender's owner comes from the directory (an owner with several Obis counts once):

- `selfAnswersPerHour` (30 unless changed): questions from one sender's owner in the last hour that obid took on to answer by itself, each counted when obid takes it on, whatever its run ends in (an answer, the owner's inbox, a decline). Past it a question goes to the owner, as one no rule allows. A question no rule covers goes straight to the owner and doesn't count.
- `selfAnswersPerHourTotal` (60 unless changed): the same, every sender together, so many senders can't spend the owner's account either. Past it a question goes to the owner too.
- Both count across a restart: each exchange keeps when obid took it on.
- `inboxPerOwner` (5 unless changed): requests of every type open in the inbox at once ("obid only: Obi-to-Obi requests in the inbox").

### obid only: Obi-to-Obi review jobs

What obid does when it accepts a `review_request` (by the owner's answer or a decision):

1. It starts a job: `kind` `review`, `repo` the request's, `title` like `Review for ernesto: fixture-app`, and two keys only obid sends on jobs: `obi` (`{"correlationId", "from", "fromOwner", "requestId"}`, null on other jobs) and `worktree` (the job's worktree, an absolute path, null before it exists; the gateway nulls it). It runs on an account Routing picks.
2. It fetches `head_branch` and `head_sha` from the repository's `remote` into its clone (whose push URL is invalid, as the runner's is), and makes a fresh worktree, `home/worktrees/<job id>`, detached at `head_sha`. When `head_sha` can't be fetched, the job fails. When the commit holds a symbolic link that leads out of the worktree, followed as the system would open it (`x -> ../../accounts`, `x -> /`, an absolute path, a chain that ends outside, or one obid can't follow), the job fails before the agent starts, naming the link (`The branch commits a link that leads out of the review's worktree ("x"), so obid didn't review it: …`): Claude Code reads inside its working folder without asking, so a link out would let the agent read what the job sandbox lets it read, its own account folder included. A link that leads to a file inside the worktree, existing or not, is fine.
3. It writes the request as data to `.obi/inputs/obi-request.json` in the worktree, and the change as data to `.obi/inputs/change.diff` (what `head_sha` changes against the fetched `base_branch` from their merge base, the files it touches listed first: `git --no-replace-objects diff --no-color --no-ext-diff --no-textconv --find-renames --patch-with-stat --src-prefix=a/ --dst-prefix=b/ origin/<base_branch>...<head_sha>`, cut at 8 MiB with a line saying so; when the base didn't fetch, a line saying obid couldn't compare). The request is `{"from", "from_owner", "type", "body"}` with `body` exactly as received. obid excludes `.obi/` in the worktree's `info/exclude`, and writes both files itself, outside the sandbox, into a worktree that holds whatever the other Obi's branch commits, so it follows no link: when `.obi` or `.obi/inputs` is there and isn't a real folder (a link out of the worktree, or a file), the job fails, saying so, and nothing is written; a link or file named `obi-request.json` or `change.diff` is removed, not written through, and the file is made new.
4. It runs the agent in the worktree with a fixed prompt that names only the commit, the conditions' kinds and the two files, never a field the other Obi wrote words into: the repository's and the branches' names (`repo`, `head_branch`, `base_branch`) reach the agent only inside the quoted file, and the prompt refers to them there by name; with the account's binary and `-p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose --permission-prompt-tool stdio --permission-mode default --setting-sources "" --strict-mcp-config --json-schema <the review output schema> --tools Read`, the environment cleaned as for any Claude Code job (no `CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_*`, `ANTHROPIC_*`, `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`), and the job sandbox below. The agent has Read and nothing else (`--tools Read`): Claude Code doesn't ask before every call (it runs read-only shell commands such as `ls`, `grep -r`, `grep -R` and `find -L`, and reads inside its working folder, without a permission prompt), so obid gives it only a tool that is safe to run unasked. Claude Code 2.1.204 has no Grep, Glob or LS in this mode; the agent reads the diff from step 3 and the files it touches. obid answers the agent's permission prompts itself and never asks the owner: after its fixed rules, only Read, and every path input must lead inside the worktree once symlinks are followed (`path`, `file_path` and `notebook_path` resolved as the system would open them, relative ones from the worktree); a path starting with `~`, a link that leads out, and an input obid doesn't know that may name a path are refused. Claude Code asks before reading outside its working folder, and before reading through a link that leads out of it. The sandbox lets the agent read its own account folder (it signs in with it), so these together keep the account out of the review: Read only, no link out of the worktree (step 2), and obid's answers for what Claude Code asks. The publish gate in step 8 is not a barrier against an account's contents: it catches what looks like a known secret, including one written spaced out, backwards, or split with dashes or zero-width characters, not every way to encode one. It closes the agent's stdin after the turn's `result` line. A review job that makes a commit fails.
5. It reads `structured_output` from the `result` line; when that is missing, the last fenced code block labeled `obi-review` in `result`, holding the same JSON. Neither, or one that doesn't match the schema, fails the job.
6. It runs the repository's `check` in the worktree, **in the same job sandbox as the agent** (the test-run limit applies): `tests_pass` is met when it exits 0. Without a `check`, `tests_pass` is not met. The check runs code from another Obi's branch, and with a remembered decision it does so without the owner looking first, so it gets nothing the agent doesn't.

7. It builds the result body (spec `protocol.md` 5.7): each finding gets the spec's recommended id (`f-` + 16 hex of SHA-256 of `file\nline\ntitle`), and findings are de-duplicated **by that id**: when two have the same id (same file, line and title, whatever their severity or detail), only the first the agent reported is kept, so ids are unique in the result as the spec requires; each acceptance condition its `met` (`custom` conditions are not judged by obid yet: not met), and `outcome` is `approved` when every condition is met, else `changes_requested`; a job that failed gives `failed` with no findings.
8. **Publish gate.** Every text field of the reply (finding titles and details, the summary) goes through the secret scan Publish uses (its rules, the vault's secrets, their encoded copies). Clean: it sends the `result`. A finding, or a scanner that can't run: it sends a `result` with `outcome` `failed`, `findings` `[]` and no text, and records that in the exchange's `publishScan`.
9. The job ends `done` (or `failed`) and publishes nothing: no push, no pull request, no check run, no comment, `outgoing` stays `[]`.
10. **Its worktree is kept** after the job ends, unchanged, so the owner (and the e2e scenario's steps 5.2, 6.3 and 8.4.3) can look at what was reviewed. The cleanup pass (`cleanup`, above) removes the worktree of a review job from another Obi (a job whose `obi` isn't null) only once that job ended more than 24 hours ago, like a settled job's, and never earlier: the rule that removes an Oby review job's worktree as soon as it has a verdict doesn't apply to it. A runner restart keeps it too.

**The job sandbox** (steps 4 and 6), bubblewrap on Linux and a Seatbelt profile (`sandbox-exec`) on macOS. It holds for the process and everything it starts:

- **Writes** go only to the job's worktree (`home/worktrees/<job id>`), the worktree's own git directory (`<common dir>/worktrees/<name>`: its `HEAD`, `index`, logs), and the job's own temporary folder, which is its `HOME` and `TMPDIR` (for the agent also its own login folder, `home/logins/<job id>`). Never an account's own folder (`configDir`, #655): no box mounts it, so nothing a job writes is ever read by an unboxed process (the tool's own sign-in, the person's own `claude`); every configured account's folder is also hidden from every box, so none reads it either, wherever it is. At its start obid looks at each configured account's folder once and logs anything there that would run something or redirect a tool the next time it runs unboxed (Claude Code's `hooks`, `env`, `apiKeyHelper`, `statusLine` and `mcpServers` in `settings.json`, `settings.local.json` or `.claude.json`; Codex's `notify`, `mcp_servers`, `model_providers` and `profiles` in `config.toml`; Cursor's `mcp.json` and a non-empty `permissions.allow` in `cli-config.json`), since a box of an older obid could have put it there. On macOS, when the Keychain holds an account's login, it takes out a `.credentials.json` an older obid copied into that folder (one with no refresh token), whether `claudeKeychain` is on or off now (#646: left there, it showed the account `signedIn` and was copied for its runs); one the Keychain doesn't hold, or one with a refresh token, may be the login itself and stays. A `configDir` that holds obid's home is refused. Nothing else is writable, the clone included: a review job makes no commit.
- **Reads, from `obid`'s `home`**: the worktree, and the clone's git common directory (`home/clones/<owner>/<name>/.git`: objects, refs, `packed-refs`, `config`, `info/`), which git needs to work in a linked worktree, read-only. Nothing else of `home`: not the database, `keys/`, `api-token`, the delegation, `plugins/`, `plugin-data/`, other clones or other worktrees.
- **Reads elsewhere**: the system's files and the tools it runs (read-only), as for any job; not the person's real `HOME`.
- **Environment**: cleaned as in step 4, with no vault secret, no code-host login (`GH_CONFIG_DIR` empty), no `obid` API token and no Obi key.
- **Network**: the check has none at all (`--unshare-net`; a profile that denies network). The agent has a network namespace of its own too, with `slirp4netns` so it still reaches its model's API (on Linux obid needs `slirp4netns` installed, Ubuntu: `sudo apt install slirp4netns`; without it no agent job and no answering run can start, and an answering run's log says only `obid couldn't answer by itself (the model's answer wasn't JSON)`) (in the stub's case, a local HTTP endpoint, at `10.0.2.2` from inside the namespace). An abstract-namespace Unix socket on the host is not in that namespace. `an_agent_reaches_its_api_but_not_an_abstract_socket` shows both. Inside the namespace the host's `/etc/resolv.conf` can't serve (on Ubuntu it names systemd-resolved's stub, `127.0.0.53`, the namespace's own loopback there, and the confined form has no `/run` for its link to lead into), so obid binds a file of its own over the one file it leads to, naming only slirp4netns's DNS forwarder, `10.0.2.3` (the host's `search` and `options` lines aren't carried over). That file is in a folder only obid's user can open and no sandbox shows (`/run/user/<uid>/obid-resolver-<uid>`, else the same name in the temporary folder), never in a folder a job writes, and it's replaced with a rename, never written through what is at its path. `a_run_with_the_network_resolves_a_host_name` resolves the model API's host in both forms, and `a_link_a_job_leaves_is_never_written_or_read_through` shows a link a job leaves is neither written nor read through.
- **No terminal, no one to run things for it.** The process leads a session of its own (`obid __session`, `setsid`; bubblewrap's `--new-session`), so it has no controlling terminal to push keystrokes into. On macOS the first process in the sandbox starts the job's program and, when that program exits or the process is signalled, kills every process in the sandbox (`kill -1`, which the profile allows only inside the sandbox). A descendant that called `setsid` ends with the job, on stop and when obid stops. On Linux, bubblewrap's PID namespace and `--die-with-parent` do the same. On macOS the profile also denies what would run or write something for the job outside its sandbox: LaunchServices opening an app (`lsopen`), Apple events, launchd jobs (`job-creation`), posted input events (`hid-control`, the window server's services), and every Unix socket but the DNS resolver's and the job's own.
- **On macOS, no preferences, pasteboard or keychain.** cfprefsd reads and writes the person's preferences for whoever asks, whatever the asker's `HOME`, `CFFIXED_USER_HOME` or file rules, so the profile denies `user-preference-read` and `user-preference-write` and the lookup of cfprefsd's services: a job has no preferences at all (CoreFoundation's writes fail; its reads find only what the job may read as files, and the person's home is hidden). It denies the pasteboard server's services (`com.apple.pasteboard.1`, Universal Clipboard's), secd's (`com.apple.securityd.*` and `com.apple.security.*`: the data protection keychain and iCloud Keychain, which secd reads and writes for whoever asks), the system keychain's securityd and SecurityAgent (which would ask the person for a keychain's password). The person's login keychain is a file in their home, which is hidden, and a client reads a file-based keychain's file itself, so it is out of reach too. Claude Code's login for a job is a `.credentials.json` in the job's own login folder, not the keychain: for an account with a `configDir`, a copy of that folder's login (the Keychain item's with `claudeKeychain`, else its `.credentials.json`) without the refresh token, in a folder that is the job's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, with the account's `.claude.json` trimmed to what keeps it signed in (copied once); when `configDir` is null, the copy of `~/.claude/.credentials.json`. Claude Code 2.1.296 no longer writes it when the Keychain is denied, so with `claudeKeychain` on (obid only: configuration) obid copies Claude Code's own Keychain item instead (`security find-generic-password -s <service> -a <user> -w`, where the service is `Claude Code-credentials` for the default folder and `Claude Code-credentials-<first 8 hex digits of the folder's SHA-256>` for an account's `configDir`): into the job's private login copy as `.credentials.json` (removed when the run ends, like a copied file). The copy carries exactly what calls the API: `{"claudeAiOauth": {accessToken, expiresAt, scopes, subscriptionType, rateLimitTier}}`, and nothing else the item holds (no refresh token, no MCP logins, plug-in secrets or device tokens). Anthropic rotates refresh tokens, so a boxed run that refreshed with a copy revoked the Keychain's own and signed the Mac's own `claude` out (2026-10-17). Instead the Mac's own Claude Code refreshes the default login (`configDir` null), unboxed: `<absolute path of claude> -p ok --model haiku --max-turns 1 --tools "" --strict-mcp-config --setting-sources "" --no-session-persistence`, with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `TMPDIR` set to a new empty private folder of obid's (Claude Code applies a `.claude.json`'s `env` from its config folder whatever `--setting-sources` says), `CLAUDE_SECURESTORAGE_CONFIG_DIR` set to empty (it names the Keychain item: empty is the default one), `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`, the kept variables and `HOME`, and no `CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_*` or `ANTHROPIC_*`, within 60 seconds, its output discarded. Claude Code 2.1.296 has no command that only refreshes (`claude auth status` reads the login without refreshing it), and it refreshes only in a token's last 5 minutes, so that is when obid asks: a keeper on a thread of its own looks every 60 seconds, while a boxed run used the default login in the last hour or a job of it still runs, and when its token ends within 5 minutes or doesn't say when, has it refreshed, reads the Keychain again and writes each running job's private copy again (only copies made from the Keychain, never through a link; a copy whose run ended isn't made again). A boxed run that starts in those 5 minutes waits for that refresh (or runs it) first. A login far from its end takes no lock; else one refresh at a time per Keychain item, at most one per 4 minutes, so many runs starting at once refresh it once and a failed refresh isn't hammered; never on obid's async runtime. After a refresh the old access token stays a secret obid redacts, next to the new one. If the refresh fails or the token has ended, the run doesn't start, saying the Mac's Claude login for that account needs signing in again. An account with a `configDir` is never refreshed by obid: no box of obid's gets that folder (#655), but on a Mac the Swift engine still runs agents unboxed with it (#655's open item), and Claude Code keeps the item's refresh lock, write lock and plaintext fallback in `CLAUDE_SECURESTORAGE_CONFIG_DIR`, which would have to be that folder. Its runs start while its token is far from its end and stop after, saying it ends soon or has ended and to run `claude` on that account. With `claudeKeychain` off, an account's login file is copied the same way, without its refresh token, and refused near its end the same way. A running Claude Code (2.1.296) reads its `.credentials.json` again when the file changes (its modification time, looked at each time it builds a client to call the model), so a long job goes on with the renewed token; a 401 alone doesn't make it read the file again when the login has no refresh token, so a job whose token ends while the keeper can't refresh it fails at its next call. obid never writes the Keychain. The value is never logged, shown or kept anywhere else. An account whose login is in neither place shows `signedIn: false` and routing skips it (#626); with `claudeKeychain` off, a login only in the Keychain is not one a job can use. Claude Code makes its Bash folder in `/tmp/claude-<uid>` unless told, so obid sets `CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR` to the job's temporary folder. The file-based keychains' securityd (`com.apple.SecurityServer`) and trustd stay reachable: Node reads the system's root certificates through them, and crashes without the first (`node::crypto::ReadMacOSKeychainCertificates`), so a Node agent such as Cursor's `cursor-agent` couldn't start.
- **Codex's login is copied without its refresh token.** Every copy obid makes of a Codex login, a job's or the brain's (`~/.codex/auth.json` into its own login folder, shown as its `~/.codex`) and a conversation's (`auth.json` into its own `CODEX_HOME`), is that file with `tokens.refresh_token` emptied (`""`: codex-cli 0.162.1 sends no credentials at all for ChatGPT tokens without that key, `401 … Missing bearer or basic authentication`). OpenAI rotates refresh tokens, so a boxed Codex that refreshed with a copy would sign the Mac's own Codex out. The tokens are learned as secrets all the same. A copy can't refresh, and obid never writes a token back: when a copied access token (its JWT's `exp`) ends within 5 minutes, or doesn't say when, the run doesn't start, with the line `The Mac's Codex login for <account> needs refreshing: run codex once.` An API-key login (no access token) runs. A job or the brain on an account with a `configDir` gets the same copy of that folder's `auth.json` (and never its `config.toml`, whose MCP servers would start inside the box with whatever tokens they carry) in a `CODEX_HOME` of its own, never the account's folder (#655); the owner refreshes it by running `codex` on that account.
- **Cursor's login is copied too, without its refresh token.** A Cursor job's `HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` (`<it>/.config`) and `CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR` are its own login folder, never the account's. Cursor runs with `AGENT_CLI_CREDENTIAL_STORE=file`, so it keeps its login as a file, never the macOS Keychain, which the box denies: `~/.cursor/auth.json` on macOS, `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/cursor/auth.json` elsewhere (cursor-agent 2026.10.01's own store; `{accessToken, refreshToken, apiKey, bedrockCredentials}`). Before every start the job gets a copy of the account's holding only `accessToken` and `apiKey` (an allowlist: never the refresh token, which a box could rotate away from the account as #651 found for Claude Code, nor Bedrock's keys), its tokens learned as secrets; a copy whose access token has ended makes the run say it isn't signed in. Once, it also gets the account's `cli-config.json`, then obid's settings over it (allow nothing ahead of obid), written inside the folder as obid opened it, never through a link put in its place (#645: the rewrite fails instead); never its `mcp.json`, whose MCP servers would start inside the box with their tokens. The account's sign-in writes that file too ([obid only: accounts](#obid-only-accounts)).

What the sandbox can't hold back, and is known:

- **macOS: other processes' arguments and environment.** Any process of the same user can read another's arguments (`KERN_PROCARGS2`), and its environment when the kernel still includes it; Seatbelt has no rule for it. So `obid` keeps every secret out of its own and its children's arguments and environment (above, "obid only: configuration"). `a_sandboxed_job_reads_other_processes_but_finds_no_secret` shows a sandboxed job reads a marker in another process's arguments and finds neither the API token nor a vault secret kept only in a file. `a_sandboxed_plugin_reads_other_processes_but_finds_no_secret` shows the same for a plug-in.
- **macOS: a keychain file outside the person's home.** securityd's file-based keychains stay reachable (above), so a keychain file the job can read, outside the hidden homes and unlocked by the person, gives up the items whose access list lets the reading program in. `a_job_reaches_no_preferences_pasteboard_or_keychain` shows the person's login keychain and secd are out of reach.
- **macOS: a job's own preferences.** cfprefsd keeps preferences in the person's home whatever the job's `HOME` says, so the profile can't give a job preferences of its own; tools that keep settings there start from their defaults every time. The same test shows preference reads and writes fail.
When `obid` can't make this sandbox on the machine, it runs neither: the job fails, and its summary says why. On macOS it tries once when it starts (`sandbox-exec` with a small profile, as bubblewrap is tried on Linux).

- **macOS: obid inside a job (its own tests).** A job that changes obid runs `cargo test -p obid` in its job sandbox, and those tests start an obid whose jobs run a fake agent. Seatbelt can't make a sandbox inside another (`sandbox_apply: Operation not permitted`, whatever the profile), so that obid's trial run is refused. When it is, and the sandbox it is in has the job profile's fingerprint, that obid runs what it starts inside the sandbox it inherited: the job's, which every process keeps and none can leave. The fingerprint, each part tried without a side effect (libsandbox's `sandbox_check` would need `unsafe`, which obid forbids): the person's home (from the password database, not `HOME`) can't be listed; a Unix socket that isn't the resolver's can't be connected to (syslog's, nothing sent); and the pasteboard server can't be reached (`pbpaste -pboard ruler`, its output discarded). Inside any other sandbox (an App Sandbox, a sandboxed launcher), or refused for any other reason, obid makes no sandbox and nothing runs, as above. Such an obid logs, when it starts, `obid runs inside a job sandbox, which Seatbelt can't nest in: what it starts stays in that sandbox`. So the test's agents write only where the job may, read nothing of the real obid's home or the person's home (no login, no Keychain), and have the job agent's network, no more; what they don't get is that obid's own narrower box (its home hidden from them, no network for its checks), which only matters to the tests that check those fences: they prove them on the Mac check, outside a job. Its first process never kills the sandbox's other processes (`kill -1` would reach the job's own agent): one that called `setsid` ends with the job. This is for the agent's own test runs: a repository whose `check` is `cargo test -p obid` can't pass, since the check has no network at all and obid's tests serve on 127.0.0.1. `a_fake_agent_test_runs_inside_a_jobs_sandbox` runs a fake-agent job test inside a sandbox made as a job's agent's and finds that log line; `a_fake_agent_inside_a_jobs_sandbox_stays_in_it` shows that test's fake agent can't list the person's home or the outer obid's, nor write outside its worktree and temporary folder; `inside_another_sandbox_a_fake_agent_test_runs_nothing` shows a sandbox that doesn't hide the person's home, and one that hides it without the job profile's other fences, run nothing.

The review output schema obid passes with `--json-schema`:

```json
{"type": "object", "additionalProperties": false, "required": ["summary", "findings"],
 "properties": {
   "summary": {"type": "string", "maxLength": 4000},
   "findings": {"type": "array", "maxItems": 50, "items": {
     "type": "object", "additionalProperties": false, "required": ["severity", "file", "line", "title", "detail"],
     "properties": {
       "severity": {"enum": ["blocker", "major", "minor", "nit"]},
       "file": {"type": "string", "maxLength": 500},
       "line": {"type": "integer", "minimum": 1},
       "end_line": {"type": "integer", "minimum": 1},
       "title": {"type": "string", "maxLength": 200},
       "detail": {"type": "string", "maxLength": 4000}}}}}}
```

Whatever the agent wrote must still pass the spec's schema before it is sent, and obid makes it so; what the agent wrote never fails the job once it matches the output schema above:

- **Findings.** A finding that doesn't pass the spec's schema for findings (a valid path, a one-line title, no control or bidirectional characters, the lengths) is dropped. Call the number dropped N.
- **The summary.** The result's `summary` is the spec's `block_text`, 1 to 4,000 characters (counted in Unicode code points, as JSON Schema counts them). obid builds it from the agent's `summary` in this order:
  1. Each CR LF, then each remaining CR, becomes LF.
  2. Every other character `block_text` forbids is removed: U+0000 to U+0008, U+000B to U+001F, U+007F, U+202A to U+202E and U+2066 to U+2069.
  3. When N is more than 0, the note is `obid dropped N finding(s) that didn't match the spec's schema.`, with N as a number; otherwise there is no note.
  4. The summary is the cleaned text, then (when there is a note and the cleaned text isn't empty) a blank line, then the note. When that is longer than 4,000 characters, the cleaned text is cut to the first `4,000 − 1 − (the rest's length)` characters and followed by `…` (U+2026), so the summary is exactly 4,000 characters and the note is whole.
  5. An empty summary (the cleaned text and the note both empty) is left out of the result: the field is optional.

  The publish gate (step 8) scans the summary as built here. With nothing to clean and nothing dropped, the summary is exactly the agent's.

**What obid sends in an exchange it was asked in, and in what order.** First `accept` (body `{}`, or `{"by_rule": "<decision id>"}` when a remembered decision accepted it) or `decline`; after an `accept`, any number of `status` with `state` `queued` or `working` and no `note`; then one `result`. obid never sends a `status` before its `accept` or `decline`: a request waiting in the owner's inbox sends nothing back. The protocol allows a `status` there (spec `protocol.md` section 4); obid doesn't send one, so the asking Obi learns nothing about the owner's inbox, and the e2e scenario's steps 5.4 and 7.3 rely on that order.

### obid only: plug-ins

Context providers and event sources (docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, "Plug-ins"). None is enabled until the owner enables it.

**`GET /v1/obid/plugins`**: every plug-in obid found, enabled or not:

```json
[{"id": "tetrix", "name": "Tetrix", "version": "0.1.0", "kinds": ["agent-mcp"], "enabled": false,
  "state": "disabled", "lastError": null}]
```

`state` is `disabled`, `starting`, `ready` or `failed` (it failed three times in a row, after restarts 1 and then 2 seconds after its first two failures, docs/ARCHITECTURE.md 5.3; or it couldn't be started, `lastError` says why; enabling it again starts it again). `lastError` may carry the plug-in's own words, so the secrets obid holds are taken out of it, as from its log lines; so they are from `PUT`'s `500`. With no plug-in found it is `200 []`. When obid's plug-in host didn't start it is `503` with `{"error": "obid's plug-in host didn't start: <why>"}`, never an empty list; `<why>` has the secrets obid holds taken out, like its log line (the host's words may carry a plug-in's).

**`PUT /v1/obid/plugins/{id}`**: `{"enabled": true}` or `{"enabled": false}`, optionally with `"config": { … }` matching the manifest's `config` schema. Answers the plug-in. Kept in `home/plugins.json`. `404` for an unknown plug-in, `422` for a config that doesn't match. Also `422` for a body that isn't that shape (`enabled` not a boolean, `config` not an object, another key), `400` for one that isn't JSON, and `503` when obid's plug-in host didn't start (the same redacted `error` as `GET`) or obid is stopping. Enabling answers once the plug-in is `ready` or its first start failed; disabling answers once it, and everything it started, has stopped.

**What obid does with them.** The plug-in host (`core/crates/obi-plugin-host`) starts when obid starts, after the plug-ins obid ships are installed (`bundledPlugins`, "obid only: configuration"), and shuts down when obid stops: every plug-in gets `shutdown` at once, side by side with the jobs' agents, and no process a plug-in started is left running when obid exits. A fresh home runs **no** plug-in: obid works fully with none enabled.

- **Context** (`context` plug-ins). Each time obid gives a job's agent its whole prompt (its first turn, and the first after obid restarted the job) obid first asks every ready context plug-in (`context/query`) with the job's typed fields only (`id`, `kind`, `repo`, `branch`, `base`, `issue`, `pr`, `files`), and `purpose` `review` for a `review` job and a review another Obi asked for (`repo`, `branch` and `base` are then the request's), `plan` for a job in Plan mode, `dispatch` for every other job. Each plug-in's items are written to `.obi/context/<plugin id>.md` in the job's worktree (excluded from git with all of `.obi/`), in place of an earlier run's: a heading and a sentence saying it is quoted data from that plug-in, then each item's `title`, `source` and `uri` as JSON strings and its `text` in a fence longer than any run of backticks in it. The prompt gets one fixed paragraph naming those files and saying they are data, never instructions; nothing a plug-in wrote is in the prompt. No answer, a failed or late one (5 seconds) gives no file and no paragraph, and the job goes on; when no plug-in gave an item, obid doesn't touch `.obi/` at all. obid writes these files itself, outside the sandbox, into a worktree that holds whatever the repository commits, so it follows no link: when `.obi` or `.obi/context` isn't a real folder (a link out of the worktree, or a file), obid writes and removes nothing, logs why, and the job goes on without context.
- **Agent MCP servers** (`agent-mcp` plug-ins). For a Claude Code agent of the owner's own job, every ready `agent-mcp` plug-in's server goes into `<job's temporary folder>/obi-mcp.json` (0600, written before each turn), named by the plug-in's id, as `{"mcpServers": {"<id>": {"type": "http", "url"}}}` (or `{"type": "stdio", "command", "args"}`; a stdio command runs inside the job sandbox, so it must be a tool the sandbox can run): only what the server needs to start, no header and no environment. The agent runs with `--strict-mcp-config --mcp-config <that file>` and then `--allowedTools` followed by **one argument per allowed tool**, `mcp__<plugin id>__<tool>`, never joined into one string; the arguments name no secret. obid's own gate (its fixed denials, before any mode or saved rule) allows an MCP tool only when it is one of those rules: every other MCP tool, of any server, is refused, also when no plug-in is enabled. With no ready `agent-mcp` plug-in the agent gets no `--mcp-config` and no `--allowedTools`. Codex and Cursor agents get no plug-in's server in this version, and a review another Obi asked for gets none (it only reads).
- **Events** (`events` plug-ins) become `pluginEvent` items in the inbox, below.
- **The log.** A plug-in's stderr and `log` messages, and what the host says about a plug-in, are logged under the `tracing` target `obi_plugin`. obid's log layer takes every secret obid holds out of each such line before it is written, plain and in its encoded forms (base64, standard or URL-safe, padded or not and at any offset in a longer run, hex in either case, URL-encoded, and runs of those that decode to one), as `[redacted: <name>]`; for base64 the whole run of base64 characters that holds it goes, so no fragment of it is left: the API token (`api-token`), the Obi key (`obi-key`: both its hex text, `keys/obi.key`, in either case, and its 32 bytes), the Sphere's delegation (`sphere-delegation`) and access token (`sphere-token`). obid has no vault yet (#457); the vault's secrets join the same set when it is ported. No secret is in obid's or any of its children's environment or arguments (a plug-in's environment is `PATH`, `HOME`, `LANG` and `TMPDIR` only).

**Plug-in events in the inbox.** Each event an `events` plug-in emits (`events/emit`, docs/ARCHITECTURE.md 5.4) that obid accepts becomes one inbox item of kind **`pluginEvent`**; an event with an `id` that plug-in sent before makes nothing new:

```json
{"id": "ev-tetrix-0b9c2d4e6f8a1b3c", "kind": "pluginEvent", "job": "", "title": "Review requested: obi-e2e/fixture-app #12",
 "text": "The tetrix plug-in says a review was requested on obi-e2e/fixture-app pull request #12.",
 "options": ["Dismiss"], "details": ["Mark it read."],
 "recommended": null, "created": "2026-10-14T12:00:11Z", "done": null, "later": null, "answer": null, "state": "open",
 "plugin": {"id": "tetrix", "eventId": "…", "kind": "pull_request.review_requested", "occurredAt": "2026-10-14T12:00:09Z",
            "repo": "obi-e2e/fixture-app", "pr": 12, "issue": null, "headSha": null,
            "quoted": {"actor": "…", "title": "…", "url": "…"}}}
```

- `id` is `ev-<plug-in id>-` and the first 16 hex of SHA-256 of the event's `id`.
- `title` and `text` come from typed fields only: the plug-in's id, the event's `kind` (a fixed phrase per kind: `Review requested`, `Changes requested`, `Issue assigned`, `Check failed`, `Mentioned`), `repo`, `pr` or `issue`. `title` is `<phrase>: <repo> #<pr or issue>` (no `#…` when the event has neither); `text` is `The <plug-in id> plug-in says <what> on <where>.`, where `<what>` is `a review was requested`, `changes were requested`, `an issue was assigned`, `a check failed` or `you were mentioned`, and `<where>` is `<repo> pull request #<pr>`, else `<repo> issue #<issue>`, else `<repo> at <the first 7 of headSha>`, else `<repo>`. `plugin.quoted` holds the event's text (`actor`, `data.title`, `data.url`) unchanged: shown as quoted text, never as a link that opens by itself, never to a model as instructions. Only `pluginEvent` items have the `plugin` key.
- The one answer is `Dismiss` (`{"done": true}` too; any other answer is `422`). `{"later": …}` puts it off as for any item. An event never starts work and no remembered decision applies to it in this version; acting on it is the owner's own step. Answering never sends anything anywhere.
- An item is never made twice for the same plug-in and event `id`, also across restarts of the plug-in or obid, and also once the first is done.
- At most 100 open `pluginEvent` items per plug-in: past that, the oldest open one is settled by itself with no `answer`. Oldest is by the order the events came in, also among events that came in the same second; `GET /v1/inbox` lists open items in that order too.

### obid only: artifacts

What an agent makes to be looked at (docs/conversation-harness.md, section 4): a page, a document, an image, a PDF, a canvas of pages. obid keeps every version of each in `home/artifacts/<id>/<version>/` (0700, files 0600), described by `home/artifacts/<id>/artifact.json`, which is what the API shows:

```json
{"id": "3f0c…", "title": "Landing page", "kind": "html", "conversation": null, "job": "79f64f22",
 "created": "2026-10-17T09:12:00Z",
 "versions": [{"version": 1, "created": "2026-10-17T09:12:00Z", "files": ["index.html", "style.css"], "bytes": 1832}]}
```

- `id`: a lower-case UUID. `title`: one line of 1 to 100 characters. `conversation` and `job`: what made it, either may be null. `versions`: oldest first, numbered from 1; each lists its `files` (paths inside the version, sorted) and their `bytes`.
- **Kinds**, from its shape: `html`, a folder with an `index.html` (a single `.html` or `.htm` file is kept as one, named `index.html`); `markdown`, a `.md` or `.markdown` file; `image`, a `.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.gif`, `.webp` or `.svg` file; `pdf`, a `.pdf` file; `canvas`, a folder with a `canvas.json`, a list of frames `[{"title": "Phone", "file": "phone.html", "x": 0, "y": 0, "w": 390, "h": 844}]`, each `file` an HTML file of the folder. Anything else isn't published, saying what it can be.
- **Made** by a job: when it ends (done, failed or stopped; not when obid shuts down mid-job), each entry of its worktree's `.obi/artifacts/` (a file or a folder, hidden names left out), the first 20 by name of them all, is published as an artifact of that job (the rest are one line in `artifactProblems`, `5 more entries: Not published: a job publishes at most 20 artifacts.`; a folder holding more than 2000 names, hidden ones too, publishes none: `Not published: .obi/artifacts holds more than 2000 names, so none of it is.`, and obid stops reading it there), titled by its name without the extension, with `conversation` the job's. Its next end gives the same entry a new version, and the same files again change nothing. Agents are told to put there what they want shown. `.obi/` is never committed. The conversation's `oby_artifact` tool (docs/conversation-harness.md, section 3) publishes from the conversation's room the same way.
- **What is copied.** Only from inside the folder obid names (a job's worktree, a conversation's room): a path that's absolute or holds `..` is refused. Every folder and file on the way and inside is opened without following a link, inside the folder already opened, so a link anywhere (`.obi/artifacts/away.md -> /etc/hosts`, a folder inside that's a link) is refused, not followed, and so is anything that isn't a plain file or folder (a named pipe, a device). A file with another link to it (a hard link, which may be to a file outside the folder) is refused, and so is anything on another disk than the folder. Names starting with `.` are left out, but count; a name with `\` or `:`, or that isn't UTF-8, is refused. At most 2000 files and folders (hidden ones too), 32 folders deep and 50 MB per version, and 50 versions per artifact: past it, a new version is refused before anything is copied or scanned (`… has 50 versions, the most obid keeps; publish it as a new one.`). A job's artifacts hold at most 500 MB, every version of each counted: an entry that would take it past is refused, with the entries after it, in one line of `artifactProblems` (`3 entries: Not published: this job's artifacts hold 498 MB, and 500 MB is the most one job's may.`). A conversation's own artifacts (those with no `job`, from `oby_artifact`) have the same 500 MB between them: `Not published: this conversation's artifacts hold … MB, and 500 MB is the most one conversation's may.` Each file is checked again by its name after it's opened (`fstatat`, not following a link): another file in its place since (`… changed while obid copied it`) or a second link to it is refused. There is no cap on the store as a whole yet.
- **The secret scan.** Before anything is kept, every file goes through the publish gate's scan (`crate::scan`, obid only: publishing the owner's work): the built-in rules, the secrets obid holds and the vault's in every form it knows, and gitleaks when it's installed, on every file read as text whatever its bytes (bytes that aren't UTF-8 read as U+FFFD, so the ASCII a key is written in stays whole), and once more with its NUL bytes taken out when it has some, so UTF-16 text is read too. What a compressed stream holds isn't looked inside: a PDF's Flate streams, a PNG's `zTXt` or `iTXt` chunks, gzip or zip content. A finding, or a scan that can't run (the vault can't be read), and nothing is kept: `Not published: the secret scan found what looks like a secret in config.js line 1 (AWS access key).` Never the value.
- **Names are scanned too.** The path asked for, the title and every file's path inside the artifact go through the built-in rules, the secrets obid holds and gitleaks, each as a line of its own, and so does every part of each between `/`s and every piece of a part between `.`s (gitleaks finds a key only where a word ends, so `<key>.md` and `<key>/index.html` would hide it); one that looks like a secret, and nothing is kept. A name, a title or a refusal that holds a run of 32 or more letters, digits, `_`, `+` or `-` with both letters and digits in it is refused the same way, as a token joined to a name (`notes-<key>.md`, `notes~<key>.md`, `<key>@x.md`) gets past gitleaks; one of obid's own ids (a UUID, `8-4-4-4-12` hex) isn't, nor is a run of lower-case hex alone (a bundler's hashed name, `main.<32 hex>.js`). A refusal never repeats such a name: any refusal (`Err`, and each line of a job's `artifactProblems`) that holds what looks like a secret, read part by part the same way, is said as `Not published: a name in it looks like a secret, so obid doesn't say it.` instead, and a job's line for such an entry is that sentence alone, never with the entry's name before it. A file that starts like a PDF, a zip or a gzip is still read by gitleaks; one it sniffs as a tar or an ISO image further in makes the scan one that couldn't run, so nothing is kept (publishing the owner's work, above, says how).
- **For whoever writes the publishing call** (the conversation's `oby_artifact`): `Artifacts::publish(Publish {root, path, title, kind, conversation, job, artifact})` in `core/crates/obid/src/artifacts.rs` answers `Ok((id, version))` once the `artifact` event went out, or `Err` with one line starting `Not published: `. `root` is obid's own folder (the room), `path` the agent's, relative to it; `kind` `None` takes it from the shape, and one given must be it; `artifact` asks for a new version of that one, which must have the same `conversation` and `job` and the same kind, and keeps its title (the owner may have renamed it). `shared.artifacts` holds it.

**`GET /v1/artifacts?conversation=&job=`**: every artifact, newest first, or only a conversation's or a job's; `[]` when none.

**`GET /v1/artifacts/{id}`**: the artifact; `404` when there's no such one.

**`GET /v1/artifacts/{id}/{version}/{path}`**: one of the version's files, `path` as `files` lists it (`assets/site.css`); anything else (another name, `..`, a hidden name, a version that isn't there) is `404`. `Content-Type` by its extension (`text/html; charset=utf-8`, `text/css; charset=utf-8`, `text/javascript; charset=utf-8`, `text/markdown; charset=utf-8`, `image/png`, `application/pdf`…; `application/octet-stream` when unknown), and on every file:

```
Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self' data: blob:; script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' data: blob:; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' data:; img-src 'self' data: blob:; connect-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; sandbox allow-scripts
X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff
Referrer-Policy: no-referrer
```

A page's scripts run, but:

- it loads only its own files (any file of obid's served on the same address, but every one needs the token) and `data:`/`blob:` URLs: no other server, no font or script from the web;
- `connect-src 'none'`: no `fetch`, XHR, WebSocket or EventSource, not even to obid's own API;
- `sandbox allow-scripts`: the page has an origin of its own, not obid's, so it can't read what obid's other pages keep (the web app's token in session storage), its cookies, or a frame of the web app; it opens no window or popup;
- `object-src 'none'`, `base-uri 'none'`, `form-action 'none'`: no plug-in, no `<base>` sending its links elsewhere, no form sent anywhere;
- `frame-ancestors 'none'`: no other page can frame it.

**What the policy doesn't stop**, so this isn't "no network" by itself: the page navigating itself to another address (a link, `location = …`, `<meta http-equiv="refresh">`), WebRTC, and DNS prefetch. A CSP has no rule for those. What such a request can carry is only what the page holds, since it reads nothing else; the apps must deny every navigation and load it with network access denied (the client contract in docs/conversation-harness.md, section 4). Text files are also scrubbed of the vault's secrets as every `/v1` answer is (obid only: the vault).

**`PATCH /v1/artifacts/{id}`** with `{"title": "…"}` renames it and answers the artifact: `422` for a title that isn't one line of 1 to 100 characters, `400` for another body, `404` for no such artifact.

**`DELETE /v1/artifacts/{id}`** deletes it with every version: `204`, `404` for no such artifact. Jobs keep its id in their `artifacts`.

**Through the gateway** a paired phone lists and reads them (the three `GET`s), files byte for byte with their headers; renaming and deleting stay on the loopback API. A file that isn't text (an image, a PDF) passes the gateway unscrubbed, as the API's own layer passes it: nothing takes the vault's secrets or the accounts' homes out of it there. That's accepted: it was scanned before it was kept (above).

### obid only: the web app

`apps/web` (Sangam's screens, #492) is a client of this API like the Mac and iPhone apps, for a browser on the same machine: Home, task pages, the conversation and Settings, showing obid's jobs, conversation and inbox. obid serves its built files on its own loopback address, so nothing else listens and nothing new is reachable from the network.

- **The page uses the API as every client does: with the token.** Every call it makes, the event stream included, sends `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (the stream is read with `fetch`, since `EventSource` can't send a header). The page keeps the token in the browser's session storage for its own origin, which another port on 127.0.0.1 is not, so a local server the person opens can't read it; it's forgotten when the tab closes, on sign-out, and when obid refuses it (`401`), after which the page asks for it again. **No cookie reaches `/v1`**: a request there without the token is `401`, whatever cookie or `Sec-Fetch-Site`, `Origin` or other header comes with it, since anything on the machine can send those.
- **`GET /app/`** is the page; **`GET /app/{path}`** its files (scripts, styles, the mascot from `character/` as `oby/oby.html`), read from `web` (configuration, above). `GET /app` is `308` to `/app/`. Only plain names inside that folder are served: no `..`, no hidden files, nothing a link leads out of it to (`404`). Pages carry a `Content-Security-Policy` (the app's own scripts only; the mascot's page may run its inline ones), and every file `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` and `Cache-Control: no-cache`.
- **The page's files are behind the token too.** They hold no data, but #492 keeps the page itself closed without the token, and a browser can't send a header when it opens a page. So with neither the token nor a page session, `GET` of any `/app` path is `401` with a small sign-in form (nothing of obid's, one script), and `/v1/…` is `401` as always. A `Host` that isn't a loopback name is `403` first, as for every request.
- **Signing in.** The form posts the token once: **`POST /app/sign-in`** with `token=<the token>` (`application/x-www-form-urlencoded`; spaces and line ends around it are ignored). Its script keeps the token in the page's session storage on the way, so the page has it for the API, and sends it again by itself (once in 10 seconds at most) when the form comes back while the tab still holds it, as after obid restarted. The right token answers `303` to `/app/` and opens a **page session**: 32 random bytes in hex, in `Set-Cookie: obid-page=<value>; Path=/app; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict` (no `Max-Age`: it ends with the browser's session). obid keeps only its SHA-256, in memory, at most 64 at once (another sign-in ends the oldest); it's nothing derived from the token. Anything else is the form again, `401`, with no cookie; so is a form sent from another site's page (`Sec-Fetch-Site` other than `same-origin`).
- **A page session opens only `GET` and `HEAD` of `/app` paths**, the page's own files, and nothing else. It ends when the browser signs out, when obid stops, or once 64 newer ones were opened. Browsers send the cookie to every port on 127.0.0.1 under `/app`, so a local server may receive it; all it opens there is files anyone can build from this repository.
- **`POST /app/sign-out`** ends the session the request's cookie names (none: nothing ends), clears the cookie (`Max-Age=0`) and answers `303` to `/app/`, the sign-in form again. Like signing in, it passes the gate on a loopback `Host` alone: it can only end a session. The page forgets the token first.
- What the page shows comes only from this API: `GET /v1/status`, `GET /v1/jobs`, `GET /v1/jobs/{id}`, `GET /v1/inbox`, `GET /v1/conversations`, `GET /v1/conversation`, `GET /v1/events`, `GET /v1/obid/plugins` and `GET /v1/connections`, and it acts through `POST /v1/plans`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /v1/conversations/{id}`, `POST /v1/jobs/{id}/messages`, `/stop`, `/dismiss` and `POST /v1/inbox/{id}/answer`. Screens with no endpoint behind them yet (the Obysphere, its knowledge) stay hidden behind a setting in the page and show nothing made up.
- **Settings › Connectors is read-only** (#518). It lists obid's accounts and its `github` login from `GET /v1/status`, its plug-ins from `GET /v1/obid/plugins` and its connections from `GET /v1/connections`, reading the two lists when the page opens and every 15 seconds while it's open. A `404` for a list means there is none (obid serves no connections before #444); any other answer is said under the page, never shown as an empty list. It turns nothing on or off and disconnects nothing: a connection is changed with a device's signature ([Connection](#connection)), which a browser has no key for, so each row says where it changes (a connection or a plug-in in Oby on the Mac, an account or the GitHub login on the runner's machine).
- **Many conversations, one on screen.** obid keeps many conversations ([obid only: conversations](#obid-only-conversations), #493). The side panel lists them all (`GET /v1/conversations`, paged by `cursor`, kept current by `conversation` events), pinned first, then newest first, and renames, pins and deletes them (`PATCH`, `DELETE`). The page shows one: the latest when it opens (`GET /v1/conversation` with no `id`), or the one picked in the list. Home's message box and New start one (`POST /v1/plans` with `"new": true`), which the page then shows; a reply sends the shown conversation's `id` (`conversation`), so the words never join another one, and the page keeps out every `turn` event whose `conversation` is another's. When the one it shows is deleted, it shows none, and its next words start a new one (`"new": true`). Words sent to a conversation deleted before the page heard of it are `404` and obid keeps none of them: the page takes it off the list, says so, and the next words start a new one.
- None of this is on the gateway's allowlist (Runner/Routes.swift): the web app is for a browser on the machine obid runs on. Reaching it from another device is the gateway's or a tunnel's, as for the API.
